Implement the following plan:

# Plan: Parse cargo test output for counts and filtering

## Context

`tspec test` currently streams cargo output live via `cmd.status()` and
only knows pass/fail per package. The summary shows `[PASS]`/`[FAIL]`
with no test counts. Worse, 0 tests running is reported as success.

Goals:
1. Parse `test result:` lines to extract passed/failed/ignored counts
2. Show per-package test counts in the summary table
3. Warn when 0 tests ran (likely a filter typo)
4. Filter "running 0 tests" noise from single-target execution

## Approach: Tee stdout with line parsing

Change `run_cargo` (Test mode only) to pipe stdout, read lines in a
loop, print each line live, and accumulate `test result:` lines. Return
parsed counts alongside the exit status.

### New struct: `TestResult`

```rust
/// Parsed test statistics from cargo test output
pub struct TestResult {
    pub passed: u32,
    pub failed: u32,
    pub ignored: u32,
    pub filtered: u32,
}
```

### Changes

#### `src/tee.rs` (new module — reusable tee-and-filter utility)

Generic "run command, tee stdout line-by-line, collect lines matching
a predicate" utility. Not test-specific.

```rust
pub struct TeeResult {
    pub status: ExitStatus,
    pub matched_lines: Vec<String>,
}

/// Spawn a command with piped stdout (stderr inherited),
/// print each stdout line live, and collect lines where
/// `filter(line)` returns true.
pub fn tee_stdout<F>(cmd: &mut Command, filter: F) -> Result<TeeResult>
where
    F: Fn(&str) -> bool;
```

This is reusable for any future command that needs live output +
selective parsing (e.g., build warnings, benchmark results).

#### `src/cargo_build.rs`

- Add `TestResult` struct
- Add `fn parse_test_result_line(line: &str) -> Option<TestResult>` —
  parses `test result: ok. 263 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out`
- Change `test_package()` return type: `Result<()>` → `Result<TestResult>`
- In `run_cargo()` for `CargoMode::Test`: use `tee_stdout()` with a
  filter for `test result:` lines, then parse and aggregate counts.
  stderr stays inherited (compiler errors/warnings stream live).
- Add unit tests for `parse_test_result_line`

#### `src/all.rs`

- Update `test_all()` to use new `TestResult` from `test_package()`
- Store counts in `OpResult.message` or add fields to `OpResult`
- Better: add `test_counts: Option<TestResult>` to `OpResult`

#### `src/all.rs` — `print_test_summary()`

- Show per-package counts: `[PASS]  263 passed` or `[FAIL]  2 failed`
- Show totals in footer: `Test: 2 packages, 279 passed, 0 failed`
- Fail (exit 1) when total tests across all packages is 0:
  `Error: 0 tests ran (filter may not match anything)`

#### `src/cmd/test.rs`

- Update single-package path to use `TestResult` return value
- Fail (exit 1) on 0 tests for single-package execution too

### Filtering "running 0 tests" noise

While reading stdout line-by-line, suppress lines matching:
- `running 0 tests` (when immediately followed by empty test result)
- `test result: ok. 0 passed; 0 failed; ...` when all zeros

Only suppress when the block is entirely empty (0 passed, 0 failed).
This cleans up output when `--test` targets specific files.

### What NOT to change

- Build and run modes stay on `cmd.status()` (no parsing needed)
- stderr stays inherited in all modes (compiler output streams live)
- The `--list` and `--target-names` code paths are unaffected

## Files to modify

- `src/tee.rs` (new) — reusable tee_stdout utility
- `src/lib.rs` — add `pub mod tee;`
- `src/cargo_build.rs` — TestResult, parse function, test_package return type
- `src/all.rs` — OpResult changes, test_all, print_test_summary
- `src/cmd/test.rs` — handle TestResult in single-package path

     1→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     2→use std::fs;
     3→use std::io::Write;
     4→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     5→use std::process::Command;
     6→
     7→use crate::find_paths::{
     8→    find_package_dir, find_project_root, find_tspec, get_binary_path, get_binary_path_simple,
     9→    get_package_name,
    10→};
    11→use crate::tspec::{expand_target_dir, load_spec, spec_name_from_path};
    12→use crate::types::{CargoFlags, Spec, Verbosity, flatten_config};
    13→
    14→const TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER: &str = "// Generated by tspec - do not edit";
    15→
    16→/// Built-in cargo profiles that don't require a `[profile.<name>]` definition.
    17→const BUILTIN_PROFILES: &[&str] = &["dev", "debug", "release", "test", "bench"];
    18→
    19→/// Validate that a custom profile is defined in the workspace Cargo.toml.
    20→/// Built-in profiles (dev, release, test, bench) are always valid.
    21→/// Custom profiles must have a `[profile.<name>]` section with `inherits`.
    22→pub fn validate_profile(profile: &str, workspace: &Path) -> Result<()> {
    23→    if BUILTIN_PROFILES.contains(&profile) {
    24→        return Ok(());
    25→    }
    26→    let cargo_toml_path = workspace.join("Cargo.toml");
    27→    let content = fs::read_to_string(&cargo_toml_path)
    28→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read {}", cargo_toml_path.display()))?;
    29→    let doc: toml::Value =
    30→        toml::from_str(&content).context("failed to parse workspace Cargo.toml")?;
    31→    if let Some(profiles) = doc.get("profile").and_then(|p| p.as_table())
    32→        && profiles.contains_key(profile)
    33→    {
    34→        return Ok(());
    35→    }
    36→    bail!(
    37→        "profile `{}` is not defined in {}. Custom profiles must be defined with \
    38→         `[profile.{}]` and an `inherits` field (e.g., `inherits = \"release\"`).",
    39→        profile,
    40→        cargo_toml_path.display(),
    41→        profile,
    42→    )
    43→}
    44→/// Check for spec settings that are likely misconfigurations.
    45→/// Returns a list of warning messages (printed at top and bottom of output).
    46→pub fn check_spec_misconfigurations(pkg_name: &str, spec: &Spec, pkg_dir: &Path) -> Vec<String> {
    47→    let mut warnings = Vec::new();
    48→    let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
    49→    let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
    50→
    51→    // -static with non-musl target (glibc + -static segfaults)
    52→    if has_linker_args {
    53→        let has_static = spec.linker.args.iter().any(|a| a == "-static");
    54→        let is_musl = spec
    55→            .cargo
    56→            .target_triple
    57→            .as_ref()
    58→            .is_some_and(|t| t.contains("musl"));
    59→        if has_static && !is_musl {
    60→            warnings.push(format!(
    61→                "Warning: -static linker arg without musl target for {}. \
    62→                 glibc + -static often segfaults; consider using a musl target triple.",
    63→                pkg_name
    64→            ));
    65→        }
    66→    }
    67→
    68→    // linker.args on lib-only package
    69→    if has_linker_args && !has_bin_target {
    70→        warnings.push(format!(
    71→            "Warning: linker.args ignored for {} (no binary target)",
    72→            pkg_name
    73→        ));
    74→    }
    75→
    76→    warnings
    77→}
    78→
    79→/// Print collected warnings again so they're visible after cargo output.
    80→pub fn reprint_warnings(warnings: &[String]) {
    81→    for w in warnings {
    82→        println!("{}", w);
    83→    }
    84→}
    85→
    86→pub fn warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale: bool) {
    87→    if had_stale {
    88→        println!("Warning: a stale build.rs was deleted, likely from a previous interrupted build");
    89→    }
    90→}
    91→
    92→/// Check if a build.rs file was generated by tspec.
    93→/// Matches either the marker comment or a file that only contains
    94→/// `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=` println lines (marker-less variant).
    95→fn is_tspec_generated_build_rs(content: &str) -> bool {
    96→    if content.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER) {
    97→        return true;
    98→    }
    99→    // Fallback: file only contains fn main, cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin printlns, and braces
   100→    let dominated_by_link_args = content
   101→        .lines()
   102→        .map(|l| l.trim())
   103→        .filter(|l| !l.is_empty())
   104→        .all(|l| {
   105→            l == "fn main() {"
   106→                || l == "}"
   107→                || l.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER)
   108→                || l.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
   109→        });
   110→    dominated_by_link_args && content.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
   111→}
   112→
   113→/// Remove a stale tspec-generated build.rs left behind by an interrupted build.
   114→/// Returns true if a stale file was removed. Caller should print a warning after the build.
   115→pub fn remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(pkg_dir: &Path) -> bool {
   116→    let build_rs = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
   117→    if let Ok(content) = fs::read_to_string(&build_rs)
   118→        && is_tspec_generated_build_rs(&content)
   119→    {
   120→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs);
   121→        return true;
   122→    }
   123→    false
   124→}
   125→
   126→/// Result of a successful build
   127→pub struct BuildResult {
   128→    pub binary_path: PathBuf,
   129→    /// The effective target directory base (e.g., "target/foo" if target_dir was set)
   130→    pub target_base: PathBuf,
   131→}
   132→
   133→/// Unified cargo runner for build and test operations.
   134→///
   135→/// Handles spec loading, build.rs generation, command construction, and cleanup.
   136→/// `cli_profile` is the profile from the CLI (e.g., "release", "release-small").
   137→/// `None` means debug (default). `Some("release")` is equivalent to `--release`.
   138→pub fn run_cargo(
   139→    mode: CargoMode,
   140→    pkg_name: &str,
   141→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   142→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   143→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   144→) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   145→    let verbosity = flags.verbosity;
   146→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   147→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;
   148→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&pkg_dir, tspec)?;
   149→
   150→    // Resolve actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when pkg_name is a path)
   151→    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;
   152→
   153→    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds
   154→    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);
   155→
   156→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
   157→    let build_rs_path = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
   158→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
   159→
   160→    // Load spec once (if present) and compute target_dir
   161→    let (spec, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
   162→        let s = load_spec(path)?;
   163→        let name = spec_name_from_path(path);
   164→        let td = expand_target_dir(&s, &name)?;
   165→        (Some(s), td)
   166→    } else {
   167→        (None, None)
   168→    };
   169→
   170→    // Compute paths
   171→    let target_base = match &expanded_td {
   172→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   173→        None => workspace.join("target"),
   174→    };
   175→    let binary_path = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   176→        get_binary_path(
   177→            &workspace,
   178→            &pkg_name,
   179→            spec,
   180→            cli_profile,
   181→            expanded_td.as_deref(),
   182→        )
   183→    } else {
   184→        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, cli_profile)
   185→    };
   186→
   187→    // Validate the effective profile exists before invoking cargo
   188→    let effective_profile = spec
   189→        .as_ref()
   190→        .and_then(|s| s.cargo.profile.as_deref())
   191→        .or(cli_profile);
   192→    if let Some(profile) = effective_profile {
   193→        validate_profile(profile, &workspace)?;
   194→    }
   195→
   196→    // Check for misconfigurations before running cargo
   197→    let spec_warnings = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   198→        check_spec_misconfigurations(&pkg_name, spec, &pkg_dir)
   199→    } else {
   200→        Vec::new()
   201→    };
   202→
   203→    let verb = match mode {
   204→        CargoMode::Build => "Building",
   205→        CargoMode::Test => "Testing",
   206→    };
   207→
   208→    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo subcommand
   209→    let status = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   210→        let path = tspec_path.as_ref().unwrap();
   211→        println!("{} {} with spec {}", verb, pkg_name, path.display());
   212→
   213→        // -vv: print spec resolution details
   214→        if verbosity >= Verbosity::Debug {
   215→            let rel_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path);
   216→            println!("[debug] spec: {}", rel_path.display());
   217→            let tc = if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {
   218→                format!("{} (from spec)", tc)
   219→            } else if requires_nightly(spec, mode) {
   220→                "nightly (auto-detected)".to_string()
   221→            } else {
   222→                "default".to_string()
   223→            };
   224→            println!("[debug] toolchain: {}", tc);
   225→            if let Some(td) = &expanded_td {
   226→                println!("[debug] target_dir: {} (expanded from spec)", td);
   227→            }
   228→            let eff = effective_profile.unwrap_or("debug");
   229→            let source = if spec.cargo.profile.is_some() {
   230→                "from spec"
   231→            } else if cli_profile.is_some() {
   232→                "from CLI"
   233→            } else {
   234→                "default"
   235→            };
   236→            println!("[debug] effective profile: {} ({})", eff, source);
   237→        }
   238→
   239→        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed
   240→        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
   241→        let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
   242→        if has_linker_args && has_bin_target && !had_build_rs {
   243→            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, spec)?;
   244→        }
   245→
   246→        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(spec, mode)?;
   247→        cmd.arg(mode.subcommand());
   248→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   249→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   250→
   251→        // Set spec path for tspec-build library to read in build.rs
   252→        cmd.env("TSPEC_SPEC_FILE", path.as_os_str());
   253→
   254→        apply_spec_to_command(
   255→            &mut cmd,
   256→            spec,
   257→            &workspace,
   258→            cli_profile,
   259→            expanded_td.as_deref(),
   260→        )?;
   261→
   262→        // For test mode, append -Zpanic_abort_tests to RUSTFLAGS if needed
   263→        if mode == CargoMode::Test && needs_panic_abort_tests(spec) {
   264→            let existing = cmd
   265→                .get_envs()
   266→                .find(|(k, _)| k == &"RUSTFLAGS")
   267→                .and_then(|(_, v)| v)
   268→                .map(|v| v.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   269→                .unwrap_or_default();
   270→            let new_flags = if existing.is_empty() {
   271→                "-Zpanic_abort_tests".to_string()
   272→            } else {
   273→                format!("{} -Zpanic_abort_tests", existing)
   274→            };
   275→            cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", new_flags);
   276→        }
   277→
   278→        // Pass global flags through to cargo (-v/-vv, -j N, extra args, etc.)
   279→        flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   280→
   281→        // -v: print command line and env vars
   282→        print_verbose_command(&cmd, verbosity);
   283→
   284→        cmd.status()
   285→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", mode.subcommand()))?
   286→    } else {
   287→        println!("{} {} (no tspec)", verb, pkg_name);
   288→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   289→        cmd.arg(mode.subcommand());
   290→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   291→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   292→        if let Some(p) = cli_profile {
   293→            match p {
   294→                "debug" | "dev" => {}
   295→                _ => {
   296→                    cmd.arg("--profile").arg(p);
   297→                }
   298→            }
   299→        }
   300→
   301→        // Pass global flags through to cargo (-v/-vv, -j N, extra args, etc.)
   302→        flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   303→
   304→        // -v: print command line even without spec
   305→        print_verbose_command(&cmd, verbosity);
   306→
   307→        cmd.status()
   308→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", mode.subcommand()))?
   309→    };
   310→
   311→    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)
   312→    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {
   313→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);
   314→    }
   315→
   316→    if !status.success() {
   317→        match &tspec_path {
   318→            Some(path) => {
   319→                let display_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path).display();
   320→                bail!(
   321→                    "cargo {} failed for `{}` with spec {}",
   322→                    mode.subcommand(),
   323→                    pkg_name,
   324→                    display_path
   325→                )
   326→            }
   327→            None => bail!("cargo {} failed for `{}`", mode.subcommand(), pkg_name),
   328→        }
   329→    }
   330→
   331→    if mode == CargoMode::Build {
   332→        println!("  {}", binary_path.display());
   333→    }
   334→    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);
   335→    reprint_warnings(&spec_warnings);
   336→    Ok(BuildResult {
   337→        binary_path,
   338→        target_base,
   339→    })
   340→}
   341→
   342→/// Build a package with a spec, returns the binary path on success.
   343→pub fn build_package(
   344→    pkg_name: &str,
   345→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   346→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   347→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   348→) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   349→    run_cargo(CargoMode::Build, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)
   350→}
   351→
   352→/// Test a package with a spec.
   353→pub fn test_package(
   354→    pkg_name: &str,
   355→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   356→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   357→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   358→) -> Result<()> {
   359→    run_cargo(CargoMode::Test, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)?;
   360→    Ok(())
   361→}
   362→
   363→/// Plain `cargo build --release` with no spec lookup.
   364→/// Used by compare to produce a baseline build.
   365→pub fn plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name: &str, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   366→    build_package(pkg_name, None, Some("release"), flags)
   367→}
   368→
   369→/// Generate a temporary build.rs with scoped linker flags from tspec.toml
   370→pub fn generate_build_rs(path: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec) -> Result<()> {
   371→    let mut lines = vec![TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER.to_string(), "fn main() {".to_string()];
   372→
   373→    for arg in &spec.linker.args {
   374→        lines.push(format!(
   375→            "    println!(\"cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin={}={}\");",
   376→            crate_name, arg
   377→        ));
   378→    }
   379→
   380→    lines.push("}".to_string());
   381→    fs::write(path, lines.join("\n")).context("failed to write build.rs")?;
   382→    Ok(())
   383→}
   384→
   385→/// Cargo operation mode
   386→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
   387→pub enum CargoMode {
   388→    Build,
   389→    Test,
   390→}
   391→
   392→impl CargoMode {
   393→    fn subcommand(&self) -> &'static str {
   394→        match self {
   395→            CargoMode::Build => "build",
   396→            CargoMode::Test => "test",
   397→        }
   398→    }
   399→}
   400→
   401→/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain.
   402→/// For Test mode, panic=abort also needs nightly because `-Zpanic_abort_tests` is nightly-only.
   403→fn requires_nightly(spec: &Spec, mode: CargoMode) -> bool {
   404→    let panic_needs_nightly = match mode {
   405→        CargoMode::Test => spec
   406→            .panic
   407→            .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some())
   408→            .unwrap_or(false),
   409→        CargoMode::Build => spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false),
   410→    };
   411→
   412→    let has_build_std = !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty();
   413→    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();
   414→
   415→    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable
   416→}
   417→
   418→/// Check if spec uses an abort-like panic mode that needs `-Zpanic_abort_tests` for testing.
   419→fn needs_panic_abort_tests(spec: &Spec) -> bool {
   420→    spec.panic
   421→        .map(|p| p.rustc_panic_value().is_some())
   422→        .unwrap_or(false)
   423→}
   424→
   425→/// Print command details at verbose level.
   426→fn print_verbose_command(cmd: &Command, verbosity: Verbosity) {
   427→    if verbosity >= Verbosity::Verbose {
   428→        let prog = cmd.get_program().to_string_lossy();
   429→        let args: Vec<String> = cmd
   430→            .get_args()
   431→            .map(|a| a.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   432→            .collect();
   433→        println!("[verbose] {} {}", prog, args.join(" "));
   434→        for (key, val) in cmd.get_envs() {
   435→            if let Some(v) = val {
   436→                println!(
   437→                    "[verbose] {}={}",
   438→                    key.to_string_lossy(),
   439→                    v.to_string_lossy()
   440→                );
   441→            }
   442→        }
   443→    }
   444→}
   445→
   446→/// Build the base cargo command (with toolchain if needed)
   447→fn build_cargo_command(spec: &Spec, mode: CargoMode) -> Result<Command> {
   448→    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   449→
   450→    if let Some(tc) = &spec.toolchain {
   451→        cmd.arg(format!("+{}", tc));
   452→    } else if requires_nightly(spec, mode) {
   453→        cmd.arg("+nightly");
   454→    }
   455→
   456→    Ok(cmd)
   457→}
   458→
   459→/// Apply spec parameters to a cargo command.
   460→/// `cli_profile` is the CLI-specified profile (None = debug default).
   461→pub fn apply_spec_to_command(
   462→    cmd: &mut Command,
   463→    spec: &Spec,
   464→    workspace: &Path,
   465→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   466→    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
   467→) -> Result<()> {
   468→    // Set custom target directory if specified
   469→    if let Some(td) = expanded_target_dir {
   470→        cmd.arg("--target-dir")
   471→            .arg(workspace.join("target").join(td));
   472→    }
   473→
   474→    // Handle high-level panic mode (cargo -Z flag)
   475→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   476→        && let Some(z_flag) = panic_mode.cargo_z_flag()
   477→    {
   478→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(z_flag);
   479→    }
   480→
   481→    // Handle cargo config
   482→    let has_profile = spec.cargo.profile.is_some();
   483→    if let Some(ref profile) = spec.cargo.profile {
   484→        match profile.as_str() {
   485→            "debug" | "dev" => {
   486→                // Debug/dev is default, no flag needed
   487→            }
   488→            _ => {
   489→                cmd.arg("--profile").arg(profile);
   490→            }
   491→        }
   492→    }
   493→
   494→    if let Some(ref triple) = spec.cargo.target_triple {
   495→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(triple);
   496→    }
   497→
   498→    if let Some(ref path) = spec.cargo.target_json {
   499→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg("json-target-spec");
   500→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(path);
   501→    }
   502→
   503→    for flag in &spec.cargo.unstable {
   504→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(flag);
   505→    }
   506→
   507→    // Inject --config key=value pairs (flattened from potentially nested config)
   508→    for (key, value) in flatten_config(&spec.cargo.config) {
   509→        cmd.arg("--config").arg(format!("{}={}", key, value));
   510→    }
   511→
   512→    // If no profile in spec, fall back to CLI profile
   513→    if !has_profile && let Some(p) = cli_profile {
   514→        match p {
   515→            "debug" | "dev" => {} // default, no flag needed
   516→            _ => {
   517→                cmd.arg("--profile").arg(p);
   518→            }
   519→        }
   520→    }
   521→
   522→    // Collect rustc flags
   523→    let mut rustc_flags: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   524→
   525→    // Handle high-level panic mode (rustc -C flag)
   526→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   527→        && let Some(panic_value) = panic_mode.rustc_panic_value()
   528→    {
   529→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C panic={}", panic_value));
   530→    }
   531→
   532→    // Handle high-level strip mode
   533→    if let Some(strip_mode) = spec.strip
   534→        && let Some(strip_value) = strip_mode.rustc_strip_value()
   535→    {
   536→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C strip={}", strip_value));
   537→    }
   538→
   539→    if !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty() {
   540→        // -Z build-std is a cargo flag, not rustc
   541→        let crates_str = spec.cargo.build_std.join(",");
   542→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(format!("build-std={}", crates_str));
   543→    }
   544→
   545→    for flag in &spec.rustflags {
   546→        rustc_flags.push(flag.clone());
   547→    }
   548→
   549→    // Handle version script (generates file and adds linker arg)
   550→    if let Some(vs) = &spec.linker.version_script {
   551→        let vs_dir = match expanded_target_dir {
   552→            Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   553→            None => workspace.join("target"),
   554→        };
   555→        let _ = fs::create_dir_all(&vs_dir);
   556→        let version_script_path = vs_dir.join("tspec-version.script");
   557→
   558→        // Generate version script: { global: sym1; sym2; local: *; };
   559→        let globals = vs.global.join("; ");
   560→        let content = format!("{{ global: {}; local: {}; }};", globals, vs.local);
   561→
   562→        let mut f =
   563→            fs::File::create(&version_script_path).context("failed to create version script")?;
   564→        writeln!(f, "{}", content)?;
   565→
   566→        rustc_flags.push(format!(
   567→            "-C link-arg=-Wl,--version-script={}",
   568→            version_script_path.display()
   569→        ));
   570→    }
   571→
   572→    // Apply rustc flags (linker args from Args handled by generated build.rs)
   573→    if !rustc_flags.is_empty() {
   574→        cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", rustc_flags.join(" "));
   575→    }
   576→
   577→    Ok(())
   578→}

   714→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");

   721→        // BTreeMap is sorted, so codegen-units < lto < opt-level

     1→//! Batch operations on all workspace packages
     2→//!
     3→//! Provides build_all, run_all, test_all for operating on all workspace members.
     4→
     5→use std::process::ExitCode;
     6→
     7→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     8→
     9→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
    10→use crate::cargo_build::{build_package, test_package};
    11→use crate::compare::{SpecResult, compare_specs, print_comparison};
    12→use crate::find_paths::find_tspecs;
    13→use crate::run::run_binary;
    14→use crate::tspec::spec_name_from_path;
    15→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    16→use crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;

    18→
    19→/// Normalize tspec patterns for per-package matching in all-packages mode.
    20→///
    21→/// Strips directory components so shell-expanded paths from the workspace root
    22→/// don't leak into sub-packages. Filters out non-tspec shell expansions
    23→/// (e.g. `target/`, `tools/`) — only `.ts.toml` files and glob patterns are kept.
    24→///
    25→/// Returns `None` if all patterns were filtered out (likely shell expansion of a
    26→/// glob that matched non-tspec entries). The caller should warn about quoting.
    27→fn normalize_tspec_patterns(patterns: &[String]) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    28→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    29→        return Some(Vec::new());
    30→    }
    31→    let filenames: Vec<String> = patterns
    32→        .iter()
    33→        .filter_map(|p| {
    34→            let name = Path::new(p)
    35→                .file_name()
    36→                .map(|f| f.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
    37→                .unwrap_or_else(|| p.clone());
    38→            // Keep glob patterns (unexpanded) and actual tspec files
    39→            let is_glob = name.contains('*') || name.contains('?') || name.contains('[');
    40→            let is_tspec = name.ends_with(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    41→            if is_glob || is_tspec {
    42→                Some(name)
    43→            } else {
    44→                None
    45→            }
    46→        })
    47→        .collect();
    48→    if filenames.is_empty() && !patterns.is_empty() {
    49→        None // all patterns were filtered — likely shell expansion
    50→    } else {
    51→        Some(filenames)
    52→    }
    53→}
    54→
    55→/// Warn that shell glob expansion likely ate the tspec pattern.
    56→pub fn warn_shell_glob_expansion(patterns: &[String]) {
    57→    eprintln!(
    58→        "Warning: -t arguments ({}) don't look like tspec files.",
    59→        patterns.join(", ")
    60→    );
    61→    eprintln!("  The shell likely expanded your glob before tspec could see it.");
    62→    eprintln!("  Quote the pattern to prevent shell expansion: -t 'pattern*'");
    63→}
    64→
    65→/// Resolve tspec patterns for a workspace member.
    66→///
    67→/// Returns the matching spec paths, or an empty vec if no patterns match.
    68→/// When `patterns` is empty, returns an empty vec (caller should use default behavior).
    69→fn resolve_specs_for_member(member_path: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    70→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    71→        return Vec::new();
    72→    }
    73→    find_tspecs(member_path, patterns).unwrap_or_default()
    74→}
    75→
    76→/// Extract a short spec label from an optional tspec path.
    77→fn spec_label(tspec: &Option<String>) -> String {
    78→    match tspec {
    79→        Some(path) => spec_name_from_path(Path::new(path)),
    80→        None => String::new(),
    81→    }
    82→}
    83→
    84→/// Result of a batch operation on a single package
    85→pub struct OpResult {
    86→    pub name: String,
    87→    pub spec: String,
    88→    pub success: bool,
    89→    pub message: String,
    90→    pub size: Option<u64>,
    91→}
    92→
    93→/// Build all workspace packages (excluding build tools)
    94→///
    95→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
    96→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
    97→pub fn build_all(
    98→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
    99→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   100→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   101→    strip: bool,
   102→    fail_fast: bool,
   103→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   104→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   105→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   106→        Some(n) => n,
   107→        None => {
   108→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   109→            return Vec::new();
   110→        }
   111→    };
   112→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   113→
   114→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   115→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   116→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   117→            continue;
   118→        }
   119→
   120→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   121→
   122→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   123→            vec![None]
   124→        } else {
   125→            specs
   126→                .into_iter()
   127→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   128→                .collect()
   129→        };
   130→
   131→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   132→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   133→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   134→                Ok(build_result) => {
   135→                    if strip
   136→                        && member.has_binary
   137→                        && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)
   138→                    {
   139→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   140→                    }
   141→                    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path).ok();
   142→                    OpResult {
   143→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   144→                        spec,
   145→                        success: true,
   146→                        message: format!("{}", build_result.binary_path.display()),
   147→                        size,
   148→                    }
   149→                }
   150→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   151→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   152→                    spec,
   153→                    success: false,
   154→                    message: e.to_string(),
   155→                    size: None,
   156→                },
   157→            };
   158→
   159→            let failed = !result.success;
   160→            results.push(result);
   161→
   162→            if failed && fail_fast {
   163→                return results;
   164→            }
   165→        }
   166→    }
   167→
   168→    results
   169→}
   170→
   171→/// Run all app packages sequentially
   172→///
   173→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   174→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   175→pub fn run_all(
   176→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   177→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   178→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   179→    strip: bool,
   180→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   181→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   182→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   183→        Some(n) => n,
   184→        None => {
   185→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   186→            return Vec::new();
   187→        }
   188→    };
   189→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   190→
   191→    for member in workspace.runnable_members() {
   192→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   193→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   194→            continue;
   195→        }
   196→
   197→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   198→
   199→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   200→            vec![None]
   201→        } else {
   202→            specs
   203→                .into_iter()
   204→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   205→                .collect()
   206→        };
   207→
   208→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   209→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   210→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   211→                Ok(build_result) => {
   212→                    if strip && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path) {
   213→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   214→                    }
   215→                    match run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[]) {
   216→                        Ok(exit_code) => OpResult {
   217→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   218→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   219→                            success: true,
   220→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   221→                            size: None,
   222→                        },
   223→                        Err(e) => OpResult {
   224→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   225→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   226→                            success: false,
   227→                            message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   228→                            size: None,
   229→                        },
   230→                    }
   231→                }
   232→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   233→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   234→                    spec,
   235→                    success: false,
   236→                    message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   237→                    size: None,
   238→                },
   239→            };
   240→
   241→            results.push(result);
   242→        }
   243→    }
   244→
   245→    results
   246→}

   315→
   316→/// A row for the summary table: package name, spec, and pre-formatted detail string.
   317→struct SummaryRow {
   318→    name: String,
   319→    spec: String,
   320→    detail: String,
   321→}
   322→
   323→/// Print a summary table with consistent formatting across all operations.
   324→///
   325→/// - `ws_name` — workspace/package name for the header
   326→/// - `cmd` — operation name ("TEST", "BUILD", "RUN", "COMPARE")
   327→/// - `detail_header` — column header for the detail column (e.g. "Status", "Status    Size", "Exit")
   328→/// - `rows` — pre-formatted rows
   329→/// - `footer` — footer line (e.g. "Test: 3 passed, 1 failed")
   330→fn print_summary_table(
   331→    ws_name: &str,
   332→    cmd: &str,
   333→    detail_header: &str,
   334→    rows: &[SummaryRow],
   335→    footer: &str,
   336→) {
   337→    let max_name_len = rows.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(7).max(7);
   338→    let has_spec = rows.iter().any(|r| !r.spec.is_empty());
   339→    let max_spec_len = if has_spec {
   340→        rows.iter().map(|r| r.spec.len()).max().unwrap_or(4).max(4)
   341→    } else {
   342→        0
   343→    };
   344→
   345→    println!();
   346→    print_header!(format!("{ws_name} {cmd} SUMMARY"));
   347→    if has_spec {
   348→        println!(
   349→            "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {detail_header}",
   350→            "Package",
   351→            "Spec",
   352→            nw = max_name_len,
   353→            sw = max_spec_len
   354→        );
   355→    } else {
   356→        println!(
   357→            "  {:width$}  {detail_header}",
   358→            "Package",
   359→            width = max_name_len
   360→        );
   361→    }
   362→
   363→    for row in rows {
   364→        if has_spec {
   365→            println!(
   366→                "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {}",
   367→                row.name,
   368→                row.spec,
   369→                row.detail,
   370→                nw = max_name_len,
   371→                sw = max_spec_len
   372→            );
   373→        } else {
   374→            println!(
   375→                "  {:width$}  {}",
   376→                row.name,
   377→                row.detail,
   378→                width = max_name_len
   379→            );
   380→        }
   381→    }
   382→
   383→    println!();
   384→    if !footer.is_empty() {
   385→        println!("  {footer}");
   386→    }
   387→    print_hline!();
   388→    println!();
   389→}
   390→
   391→/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)
   392→pub fn print_test_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   393→    let mut passed = 0;
   394→    let mut failed = 0;
   395→
   396→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   397→        .iter()
   398→        .map(|r| {
   399→            let detail = if r.success {
   400→                passed += 1;
   401→                "[PASS]".to_string()
   402→            } else {
   403→                failed += 1;
   404→                "[FAIL]".to_string()
   405→            };
   406→            SummaryRow {
   407→                name: r.name.clone(),
   408→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   409→                detail,
   410→            }
   411→        })
   412→        .collect();
   413→
   414→    print_summary_table(
   415→        name,
   416→        "TEST",
   417→        "Status",
   418→        &rows,
   419→        &format!("Test: {passed} passed, {failed} failed"),
   420→    );
   421→
   422→    if failed > 0 {
   423→        ExitCode::from(1)
   424→    } else {
   425→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   426→    }
   427→}
   428→
   429→/// Print a summary for build operations (OK/FAILED)
   430→pub fn print_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   431→    let mut ok_count = 0;
   432→    let mut failed_count = 0;
   433→
   434→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   435→        .iter()
   436→        .map(|r| {
   437→            let status = if r.success {
   438→                ok_count += 1;
   439→                "[ OK ]"
   440→            } else {
   441→                failed_count += 1;
   442→                "[FAIL]"
   443→            };
   444→            let size_str = r.size.map(format_size).unwrap_or_else(|| "--".to_string());
   445→            SummaryRow {
   446→                name: r.name.clone(),
   447→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   448→                detail: format!("{status}  {size_str:>6}"),
   449→            }
   450→        })
   451→        .collect();
   452→
   453→    print_summary_table(
   454→        name,
   455→        "BUILD",
   456→        "Status    Size",
   457→        &rows,
   458→        &format!("Build: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   459→    );
   460→
   461→    if failed_count > 0 {
   462→        ExitCode::from(1)
   463→    } else {
   464→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   465→    }
   466→}
   467→
   468→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   469→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   470→        format!("{:.1}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   471→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   472→        format!("{:.1}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   473→    } else {
   474→        format!("{}", bytes)
   475→    }
   476→}
   477→
   478→/// Result of a compare operation on a single package
   479→pub struct CompareResult {
   480→    pub op: OpResult,
   481→    pub specs: Vec<SpecResult>,
   482→}
   483→
   484→/// Compare all workspace packages that have binaries
   485→///
   486→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package discovers its own specs via default glob.
   487→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   488→pub fn compare_all(
   489→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   490→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   491→    fail_fast: bool,
   492→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   493→) -> Vec<CompareResult> {
   494→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   495→        Some(n) => n,
   496→        None => {
   497→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   498→            return Vec::new();
   499→        }
   500→    };
   501→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   502→
   503→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   504→        if !member.has_binary {
   505→            continue;
   506→        }
   507→
   508→        let spec_paths = if normalized.is_empty() {
   509→            find_tspecs(&member.path, &[]).unwrap_or_default()
   510→        } else {
   511→            let resolved = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   512→            if resolved.is_empty() {
   513→                continue;
   514→            }
   515→            resolved
   516→        };
   517→
   518→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   519→
   520→        let (op, specs) = match compare_specs(&member.name, &spec_paths, flags) {
   521→            Ok(spec_results) => (
   522→                OpResult {
   523→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   524→                    spec: String::new(),
   525→                    success: true,
   526→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   527→                    size: None,
   528→                },
   529→                spec_results,
   530→            ),
   531→            Err(e) => (
   532→                OpResult {
   533→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   534→                    spec: String::new(),
   535→                    success: false,
   536→                    message: e.to_string(),
   537→                    size: None,
   538→                },
   539→                Vec::new(),
   540→            ),
   541→        };
   542→
   543→        let failed = !op.success;
   544→        results.push(CompareResult { op, specs });
   545→
   546→        if failed && fail_fast {
   547→            break;
   548→        }
   549→    }
   550→
   551→    results
   552→}
   553→
   554→/// Print a summary for compare operations
   555→///
   556→/// With a single package, just prints its comparison table.
   557→/// With multiple packages, reprints all per-package tables then an overall OK/FAIL summary.
   558→pub fn print_compare_summary(name: &str, results: &[CompareResult]) -> ExitCode {
   559→    let has_failure = results.iter().any(|r| !r.op.success);
   560→
   561→    // Reprint per-package comparison tables together
   562→    for result in results {
   563→        if !result.specs.is_empty() {
   564→            print_comparison(&result.op.name, &result.specs);
   565→        }
   566→    }
   567→
   568→    // Only show overall COMPARE SUMMARY when there are multiple packages
   569→    if results.len() > 1 {
   570→        let mut ok_count = 0;
   571→        let mut failed_count = 0;
   572→
   573→        let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   574→            .iter()
   575→            .map(|r| {
   576→                let detail = if r.op.success {
   577→                    ok_count += 1;
   578→                    "[ OK ]".to_string()
   579→                } else {
   580→                    failed_count += 1;
   581→                    "[FAIL]".to_string()
   582→                };
   583→                SummaryRow {
   584→                    name: r.op.name.clone(),
   585→                    spec: r.op.spec.clone(),
   586→                    detail,
   587→                }
   588→            })
   589→            .collect();
   590→
   591→        print_summary_table(
   592→            name,
   593→            "COMPARE",
   594→            "Status",
   595→            &rows,
   596→            &format!("Compare: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   597→        );
   598→    }
   599→
   600→    if has_failure {
   601→        ExitCode::from(1)
   602→    } else {
   603→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   604→    }
   605→}
   606→
   607→/// Print a summary for run operations (shows exit codes, not pass/fail)
   608→pub fn print_run_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   609→    let mut error_count = 0;
   610→
   611→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   612→        .iter()
   613→        .map(|r| {
   614→            let detail = if r.success {
   615→                let code = r.message.strip_prefix("exit code: ").unwrap_or(&r.message);
   616→                format!("{code:>4}")
   617→            } else {
   618→                error_count += 1;
   619→                format!("ERROR: {}", r.message)
   620→            };
   621→            SummaryRow {
   622→                name: r.name.clone(),
   623→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   624→                detail,
   625→            }
   626→        })
   627→        .collect();
   628→
   629→    let footer = if error_count > 0 {
   630→        format!("Run: {error_count} error(s)")
   631→    } else {
   632→        String::new()
   633→    };
   634→
   635→    print_summary_table(name, "RUN", "Exit", &rows, &footer);
   636→
   637→    if error_count > 0 {
   638→        ExitCode::from(1)
   639→    } else {
   640→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   641→    }
   642→}

    49→    /// Extra arguments passed after -- to the test binary (e.g., --ignored)
    50→    #[arg(last = true)]
    51→    pub test_args: Vec<String>,
    52→}
    53→
    54→impl TestCmd {
    55→    fn effective_profile(&self) -> Option<&str> {
    56→        if let Some(ref p) = self.profile {
    57→            Some(p)
    58→        } else if self.release {
    59→            Some("release")
    60→        } else {
    61→            None
    62→        }
    63→    }
    64→}
    65→
    66→impl Execute for TestCmd {
    67→    fn execute(&self, project_root: &Path, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    68→        let cli_profile = self.effective_profile();
    69→
    70→        // Resolve package: --workspace > -p/positional PKG > cwd > all
    71→        let resolved = if self.workspace {
    72→            None
    73→        } else {
    74→            match self.positional.as_deref().or(self.package.as_deref()) {
    75→                Some(pkg) => resolve_package_arg(pkg)?,
    76→                None => current_package_name(),
    77→            }
    78→        };
    79→
    80→        if self.target_names {
    81→            return list_target_names(resolved.as_deref(), flags);
    82→        }
    83→
    84→        if self.list {
    85→            return list_tests(resolved.as_deref(), &self.name_filter, flags);
    86→        }
    87→
    88→        // Build extra_args from --names, --test, and trailing args
    89→        let has_extra = !self.name_filter.is_empty()
    90→            || !self.test_target.is_empty()
    91→            || !self.test_args.is_empty();
    92→        let flags = if has_extra {
    93→            let mut f = flags.clone();
    94→            for target in &self.test_target {
    95→                f.extra_args.push("--test".to_string());
    96→                f.extra_args.push(target.clone());
    97→            }
    98→            // Name filters and test_args both go after --
    99→            // The test harness accepts [FILTERS...] as OR-matched substrings
   100→            if !self.name_filter.is_empty() || !self.test_args.is_empty() {
   101→                f.extra_args.push("--".to_string());
   102→                f.extra_args.extend(self.name_filter.clone());
   103→                f.extra_args.extend(self.test_args.clone());
   104→            }
   105→            f
   106→        } else {
   107→            flags.clone()
   108→        };
   109→
   110→        match resolved {
   111→            None => {
   112→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
   113→
   114→                // When --test targets are specified in workspace mode, only test
   115→                // packages that actually have matching files in tests/
   116→                if !self.test_target.is_empty() {
   117→                    let members = workspace.buildable_members();
   118→                    let matching: Vec<_> = members
   119→                        .iter()
   120→                        .filter(|m| {
   121→                            self.test_target
   122→                                .iter()
   123→                                .any(|t| m.path.join("tests").join(format!("{t}.rs")).exists())
   124→                        })
   125→                        .collect();
   126→
   127→                    if matching.is_empty() {
   128→                        eprintln!(
   129→                            "No packages contain test targets: {}",
   130→                            self.test_target.join(", ")
   131→                        );
   132→                        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   133→                    }
   134→
   135→                    for member in &matching {
   136→                        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   137→                        test_package(&member.name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;
   138→                    }
   139→                    return Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS);
   140→                }
   141→
   142→                let results =
   143→                    test_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.fail_fast, &flags);
   144→                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
   145→            }
   146→            Some(name) => {
   147→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
   148→                    test_package(&name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;
   149→                } else {
   150→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
   151→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
   152→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
   153→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
   154→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
   155→                        test_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, &flags)?;
   156→                    }
   157→                }
   158→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   159→            }
   160→        }
   161→    }
   162→}
   163→
   164→/// Format a cargo test target header into a human-friendly label.
   165→///
   166→/// Cargo emits headers like:
   167→/// - `unittests src/lib.rs (target/debug/deps/tspec-abc123)` → unit tests (no --test name)
   168→/// - `tests/tspec_test.rs (target/debug/deps/tspec_test-abc123)` → integration test
   169→///
   170→/// For integration tests, we show the `--test` usable name prominently.
   171→fn format_target_header(raw: &str) -> String {
   172→    // Strip the "(target/...)" suffix for cleaner output
   173→    let label = if let Some(idx) = raw.find(" (") {
   174→        &raw[..idx]
   175→    } else {
   176→        raw
   177→    };
   178→
   179→    if label.starts_with("unittests ") {
   180→        // "unittests src/lib.rs" → unit tests, not selectable with --test
   181→        format!("unit tests ({})", label.strip_prefix("unittests ").unwrap())
   182→    } else if label.starts_with("tests/") {
   183→        // "tests/tspec_test.rs" → extract basename for --test
   184→        let basename = label
   185→            .strip_prefix("tests/")
   186→            .and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix(".rs"))
   187→            .unwrap_or(label);
   188→        format!("{} (--test {})", label, basename)
   189→    } else {
   190→        label.to_string()
   191→    }
   192→}
   193→
   194→/// List available target names for `--test`.
   195→///
   196→/// Runs `cargo test --no-run` and parses stderr for "Running tests/..." lines,
   197→/// extracting the basename (without .rs) as the target name.
   198→fn list_target_names(package: Option<&str>, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   199→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   200→
   201→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
   202→    cmd.arg("test").arg("--no-run");
   203→    if let Some(pkg) = package {
   204→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(pkg);
   205→    }
   206→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   207→    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   208→
   209→    let output = cmd.output().context("failed to run cargo test --no-run")?;
   210→
   211→    if !output.status.success() {
   212→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   213→        eprintln!("{}", stderr);
   214→        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   215→    }
   216→
   217→    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   218→    let mut found = false;
   219→
   220→    println!("Available target names for --test:");
   221→    for line in stderr.lines() {
   222→        let trimmed = line.trim();
   223→        // --no-run emits "Executable tests/foo.rs (target/...)" for integration tests
   224→        if let Some(rest) = trimmed
   225→            .strip_prefix("Executable tests/")
   226→            .or_else(|| trimmed.strip_prefix("Running tests/"))
   227→        {
   228→            let file = if let Some(idx) = rest.find(" (") {
   229→                &rest[..idx]
   230→            } else {
   231→                rest
   232→            };
   233→            if let Some(name) = file.strip_suffix(".rs") {
   234→                println!("  {}    (tests/{})", name, file);
   235→                found = true;
   236→            }
   237→        }
   238→    }
   239→
   240→    if !found {
   241→        println!("  (none — no integration test files in tests/)");
   242→    }
   243→
   244→    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   245→}
   246→
   247→/// Run `cargo test -- --list` and format the output.
   248→///
   249→/// Groups test functions under their target headers, showing counts per target
   250→/// and a total. Skips targets with zero tests.
   251→fn list_tests(
   252→    package: Option<&str>,
   253→    name_filter: &[String],
   254→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   255→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   256→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   257→
   258→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
   259→    cmd.arg("test");
   260→    if let Some(pkg) = package {
   261→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(pkg);
   262→    }
   263→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   264→    cmd.arg("--");
   265→    cmd.args(name_filter);
   266→    cmd.arg("--list");
   267→    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   268→
   269→    let output = cmd.output().context("failed to run cargo test -- --list")?;
   270→
   271→    if !output.status.success() {
   272→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   273→        eprintln!("{}", stderr);
   274→        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   275→    }
   276→
   277→    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   278→    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
   279→
   280→    // Parse target headers from stderr ("Running ..." and "Doc-tests ..." lines)
   281→    // Produce human-friendly labels that show the --test name when applicable.
   282→    let targets: Vec<String> = stderr
   283→        .lines()
   284→        .filter_map(|line| {
   285→            let trimmed = line.trim();
   286→            if trimmed.starts_with("Running ") {
   287→                let rest = trimmed.strip_prefix("Running ").unwrap();
   288→                Some(format_target_header(rest))
   289→            } else if trimmed.starts_with("Doc-tests ") {
   290→                Some(trimmed.to_string())
   291→            } else {
   292→                None
   293→            }
   294→        })
   295→        .collect();
   296→
   297→    // Parse stdout into groups split by "N tests, M benchmarks" summary lines
   298→    let mut groups: Vec<Vec<&str>> = Vec::new();
   299→    let mut current: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
   300→    for line in stdout.lines() {
   301→        if line.contains("tests,") && line.contains("benchmarks") {
   302→            groups.push(std::mem::take(&mut current));
   303→        } else if line.ends_with(": test") || line.ends_with(": bench") {
   304→            current.push(line);
   305→        }
   306→    }
   307→
   308→    // Print grouped output
   309→    let mut total = 0;
   310→    for (i, tests) in groups.iter().enumerate() {
   311→        if tests.is_empty() {
   312→            continue;
   313→        }
   314→        let unknown = "unknown target".to_string();
   315→        let header = targets.get(i).unwrap_or(&unknown);
   316→        println!("{} ({} tests)", header, tests.len());
   317→        for test in tests {
   318→            // Strip ": test" / ": bench" suffix for cleaner output
   319→            let name = test
   320→                .strip_suffix(": test")
   321→                .or_else(|| test.strip_suffix(": bench"))
   322→                .unwrap_or(test);
   323→            println!("  {}", name);
   324→        }
   325→        total += tests.len();
   326→        println!();
   327→    }
   328→
   329→    println!("{} tests total", total);
   330→    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   331→}

     1→# Chores 6
     2→
     3→## 20260220 - Parse cargo test output for counts and filtering
     4→
     5→### Problem
     6→
     7→`tspec test` streams cargo output live but has no insight into what
     8→happened. The summary shows only `[PASS]`/`[FAIL]` per package with
     9→no test counts. Worse, 0 tests running reports as success — a filter
    10→typo silently passes. And `--test <target>` emits noisy "running 0
    11→tests" blocks for non-matching targets within a package.
    12→
    13→### Solution
    14→
    15→Tee stdout line-by-line (live output + selective capture), parse
    16→cargo's `test result:` lines for counts, and surface them:
    17→
    18→- Per-package counts in summary: `[PASS]  263 passed`
    19→- Aggregate footer: `Test: 2 packages, 279 passed, 0 failed`
    20→- Fail (exit 1) when 0 tests ran across all packages
    21→- Filter "running 0 tests" noise
    22→
    23→Infrastructure in a reusable `tee.rs` module so future commands
    24→(build warnings, benchmarks) can use the same pattern.
    25→
    26→### Plan
    27→
    28→- **dev1:** tee.rs + TestResult + parse_test_result_line + wire into
    29→  run_cargo for test mode. No visible behavior change.
    30→- **dev2:** Summary counts, 0-test failure, noise filtering.
    31→
    32→## 20260220 - Remove classify_package and PackageKind
    33→
    34→### Context
    35→
    36→`classify_package()` hardcoded directory conventions (`apps/`, `libs/`,
    37→`tools/`, `*/tests`) specific to one workspace layout. tspec shouldn't
    38→impose layout opinions — cargo metadata already provides `has_binary`,
    39→and the only real behavioral need is excluding build tools (tspec/xt/xtask)
    40→from batch operations.
    41→
    42→### Changes
    43→
    44→**`src/workspace.rs`:**
    45→- Removed `PackageKind` enum (5 variants) and `classify_package()` fn
    46→- Removed `test_members()` method and `is_build_tool` field
    47→- `PackageMember` now has only `name`, `path`, `has_binary`
    48→- `buildable_members()` returns all members (no exclusions)
    49→- `runnable_members()` returns all members with binaries
    50→- Replaced 11 classify tests with 1 `discover_works` test
    51→
    52→**`src/all.rs`:**
    53→- Removed `PackageKind`, `PermissionsExt` imports
    54→- Removed `PackageKind::Test` skip and entire 100-line
    55→  test-binary-discovery block from `test_all()`
    56→- `test_all()` now just iterates `buildable_members()` + `test_package()`
    57→
    58→**`tests/integration_test.rs`:**
    59→- Removed `#[ignore]` from all 16 integration tests — they run
    60→  by default now since they're fast and anyone running `tspec test`
    61→  has the full toolchain
    62→
    63→### Rationale for removing is_build_tool
    64→
    65→The `xt`/`xtask` exclusions were vestigial from the rlibc-x workspace.
    66→Excluding `tspec` from its own `tspec test` was counterproductive —
    67→`tspec test` only showed tspec-build in the summary. No workspace
    68→layout opinions should be baked into tspec.
    69→
    70→### Behavioral changes
    71→
    72→- `tspec test` (all-packages mode) now includes all workspace members
    73→- `runnable_members()` returns any member with a binary (no exclusions)
    74→- Integration tests run by default (no `-- --ignored` needed)
    75→
    76→## 20260219 - Test infrastructure: fixture workspaces and test args
    77→
    78→### Context
    79→
    80→tspec has 255 unit tests but zero integration tests that actually build real packages.
    81→The top todo item is "Add a permanent test workspace for integration testing."
    82→
    83→Three project structures need test coverage:
    84→1. **POP** — Plain Old Package (single Cargo.toml, no workspace)
    85→2. **POP+WS** — POP with embedded workspace (like tspec itself)
    86→3. **POWS** — Pure Old Workspace (workspace root, no package)
    87→
    88→### Plan

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/tee.rs",
  "content": "//! Tee utility: run a command, print stdout live, collect matching lines.\n\nuse anyhow::{Context, Result};\nuse std::io::{BufRead, BufReader};\nuse std::process::{Command, ExitStatus, Stdio};\n\n/// Result of a tee'd command execution.\npub struct TeeResult {\n    pub status: ExitStatus,\n    pub matched_lines: Vec<String>,\n}\n\n/// Spawn a command with piped stdout (stderr inherited),\n/// print each stdout line live, and collect lines where\n/// `filter(line)` returns true.\npub fn tee_stdout<F>(cmd: &mut Command, filter: F) -> Result<TeeResult>\nwhere\n    F: Fn(&str) -> bool,\n{\n    let mut child = cmd\n        .stdout(Stdio::piped())\n        .stderr(Stdio::inherit())\n        .spawn()\n        .context(\"failed to spawn command\")?;\n\n    let stdout = child.stdout.take().expect(\"stdout was piped\");\n    let reader = BufReader::new(stdout);\n\n    let mut matched_lines = Vec::new();\n\n    for line in reader.lines() {\n        let line = line.context(\"failed to read stdout line\")?;\n        if filter(&line) {\n            matched_lines.push(line.clone());\n        }\n        println!(\"{}\", line);\n    }\n\n    let status = child.wait().context(\"failed to wait for command\")?;\n\n    Ok(TeeResult {\n        status,\n        matched_lines,\n    })\n}\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cargo_build.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Unified cargo runner for build and test operations.",
  "new_string": "/// Parsed test statistics from cargo test output.\n#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone)]\npub struct TestResult {\n    pub passed: u32,\n    pub failed: u32,\n    pub ignored: u32,\n    pub filtered: u32,\n}\n\nimpl TestResult {\n    /// Aggregate another TestResult into this one.\n    pub fn merge(&mut self, other: &TestResult) {\n        self.passed += other.passed;\n        self.failed += other.failed;\n        self.ignored += other.ignored;\n        self.filtered += other.filtered;\n    }\n\n    /// Total tests that actually ran (passed + failed).\n    pub fn total_ran(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.passed + self.failed\n    }\n}\n\n/// Parse a `test result:` line from cargo test output.\n///\n/// Example: `test result: ok. 263 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.42s`\npub fn parse_test_result_line(line: &str) -> Option<TestResult> {\n    let rest = line.strip_prefix(\"test result:\")?;\n    let rest = rest.trim();\n    // Skip the \"ok.\" or \"FAILED.\" prefix\n    let rest = rest\n        .strip_prefix(\"ok.\")\n        .or_else(|| rest.strip_prefix(\"FAILED.\"))?;\n    let rest = rest.trim();\n\n    let mut result = TestResult::default();\n    for part in rest.split(';') {\n        let part = part.trim();\n        if let Some((num, label)) = part.split_once(' ') {\n            let n: u32 = num.parse().ok()?;\n            match label.trim() {\n                \"passed\" => result.passed = n,\n                \"failed\" => result.failed = n,\n                \"ignored\" => result.ignored = n,\n                \"filtered\" | \"filtered out\" => result.filtered = n,\n                _ => {} // \"measured\", \"finished in ...\"\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    Some(result)\n}\n\n/// Unified cargo runner for build and test operations."
}
```

   187→
   188→/// Unified cargo runner for build and test operations.
   189→///
   190→/// Handles spec loading, build.rs generation, command construction, and cleanup.
   191→/// `cli_profile` is the profile from the CLI (e.g., "release", "release-small").
   192→/// `None` means debug (default). `Some("release")` is equivalent to `--release`.
   193→pub fn run_cargo(
   194→    mode: CargoMode,
   195→    pkg_name: &str,
   196→    tspec: Option<&str>,
   197→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   198→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   199→) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   200→    let verbosity = flags.verbosity;
   201→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   202→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;
   203→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&pkg_dir, tspec)?;
   204→
   205→    // Resolve actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when pkg_name is a path)
   206→    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;
   207→
   208→    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds
   209→    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);
   210→
   211→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
   212→    let build_rs_path = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
   213→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
   214→
   215→    // Load spec once (if present) and compute target_dir
   216→    let (spec, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
   217→        let s = load_spec(path)?;
   218→        let name = spec_name_from_path(path);
   219→        let td = expand_target_dir(&s, &name)?;
   220→        (Some(s), td)
   221→    } else {
   222→        (None, None)
   223→    };
   224→
   225→    // Compute paths
   226→    let target_base = match &expanded_td {
   227→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   228→        None => workspace.join("target"),
   229→    };
   230→    let binary_path = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   231→        get_binary_path(
   232→            &workspace,
   233→            &pkg_name,
   234→            spec,
   235→            cli_profile,
   236→            expanded_td.as_deref(),
   237→        )
   238→    } else {
   239→        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, cli_profile)

     };\n            println!(\"[debug] toolchain: {}\", tc);\n            if let Some(td) = &expanded_td {\n                println!(\"[debug] target_dir: {} (expanded from spec)\", td);\n            }\n            let eff = effective_profile.unwrap_or(\"debug\");\n            let source = if spec.cargo.profile.is_some() {\n                \"from spec\"\n            } else if cli_profile.is_some() {\n                \"from CLI\"\n            } else {\n                \"default\"\n            };\n            println!(\"[debug] effective profile: {} ({})\", eff, source);\n        }\n\n        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed\n        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();\n        let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join(\"src/main.rs\").exists();\n        if has_linker_args && has_bin_target && !had_build_rs {\n            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, spec)?;\n        }\n\n        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(spec, mode)?;\n        cmd.arg(mode.subcommand());\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(&pkg_name);\n        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);\n\n        // Set spec path for tspec-build library to read in build.rs\n        cmd.env(\"TSPEC_SPEC_FILE\", path.as_os_str());\n\n        apply_spec_to_command(\n            &mut cmd,\n            spec,\n            &workspace,\n            cli_profile,\n            expanded_td.as_deref(),\n        )?;\n\n        // For test mode, append -Zpanic_abort_tests to RUSTFLAGS if needed\n        if mode == CargoMode::Test && needs_panic_abort_tests(spec) {\n            let existing = cmd\n                .get_envs()\n                .find(|(k, _)| k == &\"RUSTFLAGS\")\n                .and_then(|(_, v)| v)\n                .map(|v| v.to_string_lossy().into_owned())\n                .unwrap_or_default();\n            let new_flags = if existing.is_empty() {\n                \"-Zpanic_abort_tests\".to_string()\n            } else {\n                format!(\"{} -Zpanic_abort_tests\", existing)\n            };\n            cmd.env(\"RUSTFLAGS\", new_flags);\n        }\n\n        // Pass global flags through to cargo (-v/-vv, -j N, extra args, etc.)\n        flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);\n\n        // -v: print command line and env vars\n        print_verbose_command(&cmd, verbosity);\n\n        cmd\n    } else {\n        println!(\"{} {} (no tspec)\", verb, pkg_name);\n        let mut cmd = Command::new(\"cargo\");\n        cmd.arg(mode.subcommand());\n        cmd.arg(\"-p\").arg(&pkg_name);\n        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);\n        if let Some(p) = cli_profile {\n            match p {\n                \"debug\" | \"dev\" => {}\n                _ => {\n                    cmd.arg(\"--profile\").arg(p);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Pass global flags through to cargo (-v/-vv, -j N, extra args, etc.)\n        flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);\n\n        // -v: print command line even without spec\n        print_verbose_command(&cmd, verbosity);\n\n        cmd\n    };\n\n    // Execute the command — tee stdout for test mode to capture result lines\n    let (status, test_result) = if mode == CargoMode::Test {\n        let tee = tee_stdout(&mut cmd, |line| line.starts_with(\"test result:\"))\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"failed to run cargo {}\", mode.subcommand()))?;\n        let mut aggregated = TestResult::default();\n        for line in &tee.matched_lines {\n            if let Some(parsed) = parse_test_result_line(line) {\n                aggregated.merge(&parsed);\n            }\n        }\n        (tee.status, Some(aggregated))\n    } else {\n        let s = cmd\n            .status()\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"failed to run cargo {}\", mode.subcommand()))?;\n        (s, None)\n    };\n\n    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)\n    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {\n        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);\n    }\n\n    if !status.success() {\n        match &tspec_path {\n            Some(path) => {\n                let display_path = path.strip_prefix(&workspace).unwrap_or(path).display();\n                bail!(\n                    \"cargo {} failed for `{}` with spec {}\",\n                    mode.subcommand(),\n                    pkg_name,\n                    display_path\n                )\n            }\n            None => bail!(\"cargo {} failed for `{}`\", mode.subcommand(), pkg_name),\n        }\n    }\n\n    if mode == CargoMode::Build {\n        println!(\"  {}\", binary_path.display());\n    }\n    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);\n    reprint_warnings(&spec_warnings);\n    Ok((\n        BuildResult {\n            binary_path,\n            target_base,\n        },\n        test_result,\n    ))\n}\n\n/// Build a package with a spec, returns the binary path on success.\npub fn build_package(\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n    cli_profile: Option<&str>,\n    flags: &CargoFlags,\n) -> Result<BuildResult> {\n    let (build_result, _) = run_cargo(CargoMode::Build, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)?;\n    Ok(build_result)\n}\n\n/// Test a package with a spec, returns parsed test counts.\npub fn test_package(\n    pkg_name: &str,\n    tspec: Option<&str>,\n    cli_profile: Option<&str>,\n    flags: &CargoFlags,\n) -> Result<TestResult> {\n    let (_, test_result) = run_cargo(CargoMode::Test, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)?;\n    Ok(test_result.unwrap_or_default())\n}"
}

0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.42s\";\n        let r = parse_test_result_line(line).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(r.passed, 263);\n        assert_eq!(r.failed, 0);\n        assert_eq!(r.ignored, 0);\n        assert_eq!(r.filtered, 0);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_test_result_failed() {\n        let line = \"test result: FAILED. 10 passed; 2 failed; 1 ignored; 0 measured; 5 filtered out; finished in 1.23s\";\n        let r = parse_test_result_line(line).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(r.passed, 10);\n        assert_eq!(r.failed, 2);\n        assert_eq!(r.ignored, 1);\n        assert_eq!(r.filtered, 5);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_test_result_all_zeros() {\n        let line =\n            \"test result: ok. 0 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.00s\";\n        let r = parse_test_result_line(line).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(r.passed, 0);\n        assert_eq!(r.failed, 0);\n        assert_eq!(r.total_ran(), 0);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_test_result_not_a_result_line() {\n        assert!(parse_test_result_line(\"running 42 tests\").is_none());\n        assert!(parse_test_result_line(\"test foo::bar ... ok\").is_none());\n        assert!(parse_test_result_line(\"\").is_none());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_result_merge() {\n        let mut a = TestResult {\n            passed: 10,\n            failed: 1,\n            ignored: 2,\n            filtered: 3,\n        };\n        let b = TestResult {\n            passed: 5,\n            failed: 0,\n            ignored: 1,\n            filtered: 2,\n        };\n        a.merge(&b);\n        assert_eq!(a.passed, 15);\n        assert_eq!(a.failed, 1);\n        assert_eq!(a.ignored, 3);\n        assert_eq!(a.filtered, 5);\n    }\n}"

> AGENT

Good point. `TestResult` and `parse_test_result_line()` are test-specific — they don't belong in `cargo_build.rs` which is about general cargo orchestration. Moving them to `src/cmd/test.rs` makes more sense since that's where test output interpretation lives.

However, there's a dependency: `run_cargo()` in `cargo_build.rs` uses `tee_stdout` and `parse_test_result_line` to capture results. So the question is where the parsing/aggregation happens:

1. **Option A:** Keep `run_cargo` doing the tee+parse internally, but move `TestResult` and `parse_test_result_line` to `cmd/test.rs` and import them back
2. **Option B:** Have `run_cargo` return the raw matched lines (just strings), and let `cmd/test.rs` do the parsing — cleaner separation

Option B feels cleaner — `run_cargo` just tees and collects `test result:` lines as strings, and the caller (`cmd/test.rs`) parses them into `TestResult`. That way `cargo_build.rs` stays test-agnostic.

Want me to go with Option B?

> DEVELOPER
