    36→/// Resolve a `-p` argument (path or name) to the actual cargo package name.
    37→/// Returns Some(name) for a package, None if it resolves to a workspace root
    38→/// with no `[package]` section (meaning "operate on all packages").
    39→pub(crate) fn resolve_package_arg(pkg: &str) -> Result<Option<String>> {
    40→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    41→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg)?;
    42→    match get_package_name(&pkg_dir) {
    43→        Ok(name) => Ok(Some(name)),
    44→        Err(_) => Ok(None),
    45→    }
    46→}
    47→
    48→/// Helper for commands that execute a cargo subcommand.
    49→pub fn execute_cargo_subcommand(
    50→    subcommand: &str,
    51→    args: &[OsString],
    52→    project_root: &Path,
    53→    flags: &CargoFlags,
    54→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
    55→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
    56→    cmd.arg(subcommand);
    57→    cmd.args(args);
    58→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
    59→    cmd.current_dir(project_root);
    60→    let status = cmd
    61→        .status()
    62→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to run cargo {}", subcommand))?;
    63→    if status.success() {
    64→        Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
    65→    } else {
    66→        bail!("cargo {} failed", subcommand);
    67→    }
    68→}
    69→
    70→/// Check if current directory is a package (has Cargo.toml with [package]).
    71→/// Returns None at a workspace root so that commands default to all-packages mode.
    72→pub(crate) fn current_package_name() -> Option<String> {
    73→    let cwd = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;
    74→    // At a workspace root, don't treat it as a single package
    75→    if cwd.join("Cargo.toml").exists() && !is_pop(&cwd) {
    76→        return None;
    77→    }
    78→    get_package_name(&cwd).ok()
    79→}

   146→    pub fn merge(&mut self, other: &TestResult) {

   159→/// Parse a `test result:` line from cargo test output.
   160→///
   161→/// Example: `test result: ok. 263 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.42s`
   162→pub fn parse_test_result_line(line: &str) -> Option<TestResult> {
   163→    let rest = line.strip_prefix("test result:")?;
   164→    let rest = rest.trim();
   165→    // Skip the "ok." or "FAILED." prefix

   167→        .strip_prefix("ok.")

   437→    Ok(test_result.unwrap_or_default())
   438→}
   439→
   440→/// Plain `cargo build --release` with no spec lookup.
   441→/// Used by compare to produce a baseline build.
   442→pub fn plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name: &str, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   443→    build_package(pkg_name, None, Some("release"), flags)
   444→}

  "old_string": "/// Parsed test statistics from cargo test output.\n#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone)]\npub struct TestResult {\n    pub passed: u32,\n    pub failed: u32,\n    pub ignored: u32,\n    pub filtered: u32,\n}\n\nimpl TestResult {\n    /// Aggregate another TestResult into this one.\n    pub fn merge(&mut self, other: &TestResult) {\n        self.passed += other.passed;\n        self.failed += other.failed;\n        self.ignored += other.ignored;\n        self.filtered += other.filtered;\n    }\n\n    /// Total tests that actually ran (passed + failed).\n    pub fn total_ran(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.passed + self.failed\n    }\n}\n\n/// Parse a `test result:` line from cargo test output.\n///\n/// Example: `test result: ok. 263 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.42s`\npub fn parse_test_result_line(line: &str) -> Option<TestResult> {\n    let rest = line.strip_prefix(\"test result:\")?;\n    let rest = rest.trim();\n    // Skip the \"ok.\" or \"FAILED.\" prefix\n    let rest = rest\n        .strip_prefix(\"ok.\")\n        .or_else(|| rest.strip_prefix(\"FAILED.\"))?;\n    let rest = rest.trim();\n\n    let mut result = TestResult::default();\n    for part in rest.split(';') {\n        let part = part.trim();\n        if let Some((num, label)) = part.split_once(' ') {\n            let Ok(n) = num.parse::<u32>() else {\n                continue;\n            };\n            match label.trim() {\n                \"passed\" => result.passed = n,\n                \"failed\" => result.failed = n,\n                \"ignored\" => result.ignored = n,\n                \"filtered\" | \"filtered out\" => result.filtered = n,\n                _ => {} // \"measured\"\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    Some(result)\n}\n\n/// Unified cargo runner for build and test operations.",
  "new_string": "/// Unified cargo runner for build and test operations."
}

}

{

  "old_string": "use anyhow::{Context, Result};\nuse clap::Args;\nuse std::path::Path;\nuse std::process::ExitCode;\n\nuse super::{Execute, current_package_name, resolve_package_arg};\nuse crate::all::{print_test_summary, test_all};\nuse crate::cargo_build::test_package;\nuse crate::find_paths::{find_project_root, find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};\nuse crate::types::CargoFlags;\nuse crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;\n\n/// Test package(s) with a translation spec",
  "new_string": "use anyhow::{Context, Result};\nuse clap::Args;\nuse std::path::Path;\nuse std::process::ExitCode;\n\nuse super::{Execute, current_package_name, resolve_package_arg};\nuse crate::all::{print_test_summary, test_all};\nuse crate::cargo_build::test_package;\nuse crate::find_paths::{find_project_root, find_tspecs, get_package_name, resolve_package_dir};\nuse crate::types::CargoFlags;\nuse crate::workspace::WorkspaceInfo;\n\n/// Parsed test statistics from cargo test output.\n#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone)]\npub struct TestResult {\n    pub passed: u32,\n    pub failed: u32,\n    pub ignored: u32,\n    pub filtered: u32,\n}\n\nimpl TestResult {\n    /// Aggregate another TestResult into this one.\n    pub fn merge(&mut self, other: &TestResult) {\n        self.passed += other.passed;\n        self.failed += other.failed;\n        self.ignored += other.ignored;\n        self.filtered += other.filtered;\n    }\n\n    /// Total tests that actually ran (passed + failed).\n    pub fn total_ran(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.passed + self.failed\n    }\n}\n\n/// Parse a `test result:` line from cargo test output.\n///\n/// Example: `test result: ok. 263 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.42s`\npub fn parse_test_result_line(line: &str) -> Option<TestResult> {\n    let rest = line.strip_prefix(\"test result:\")?;\n    let rest = rest.trim();\n    // Skip the \"ok.\" or \"FAILED.\" prefix\n    let rest = rest\n        .strip_prefix(\"ok.\")\n        .or_else(|| rest.strip_prefix(\"FAILED.\"))?;\n    let rest = rest.trim();\n\n    let mut result = TestResult::default();\n    for part in rest.split(';') {\n        let part = part.trim();\n        if let Some((num, label)) = part.split_once(' ') {\n            let Ok(n) = num.parse::<u32>() else {\n                continue;\n            };\n            match label.trim() {\n                \"passed\" => result.passed = n,\n                \"failed\" => result.failed = n,\n                \"ignored\" => result.ignored = n,\n                \"filtered\" | \"filtered out\" => result.filtered = n,\n                _ => {} // \"measured\"\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    Some(result)\n}\n\n/// Parse and aggregate raw `test result:` lines into a single TestResult.\npub fn parse_test_results(lines: &[String]) -> TestResult {\n    let mut aggregated = TestResult::default();\n    for line in lines {\n        if let Some(parsed) = parse_test_result_line(line) {\n            aggregated.merge(&parsed);\n        }\n    }\n    aggregated\n}\n\n/// Test package(s) with a translation spec"
}

0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.42s\";\n        let r = parse_test_result_line(line).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(r.passed, 263);\n        assert_eq!(r.failed, 0);\n        assert_eq!(r.ignored, 0);\n        assert_eq!(r.filtered, 0);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_test_result_failed() {\n        let line = \"test result: FAILED. 10 passed; 2 failed; 1 ignored; 0 measured; 5 filtered out; finished in 1.23s\";\n        let r = parse_test_result_line(line).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(r.passed, 10);\n        assert_eq!(r.failed, 2);\n        assert_eq!(r.ignored, 1);\n        assert_eq!(r.filtered, 5);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_test_result_all_zeros() {\n        let line = \"test result: ok. 0 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.00s\";\n        let r = parse_test_result_line(line).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(r.passed, 0);\n        assert_eq!(r.failed, 0);\n        assert_eq!(r.total_ran(), 0);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_test_result_not_a_result_line() {\n        assert!(parse_test_result_line(\"running 42 tests\").is_none());\n        assert!(parse_test_result_line(\"test foo::bar ... ok\").is_none());\n        assert!(parse_test_result_line(\"\").is_none());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn test_result_merge() {\n        let mut a = TestResult {\n            passed: 10,\n            failed: 1,\n            ignored: 2,\n            filtered: 3,\n        };\n        let b = TestResult {\n            passed: 5,\n            failed: 0,\n            ignored: 1,\n            filtered: 2,\n        };\n        a.merge(&b);\n        assert_eq!(a.passed, 15);\n        assert_eq!(a.failed, 1);\n        assert_eq!(a.ignored, 3);\n        assert_eq!(a.filtered, 5);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_test_results_aggregates() {\n        let lines = vec![\n            \"test result: ok. 10 passed; 0 failed; 1 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.1s\".to_string(),\n            \"test result: ok. 5 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 3 filtered out; finished in 0.2s\".to_string(),\n        ];\n        let r = parse_test_results(&lines);\n        assert_eq!(r.passed, 15);\n        assert_eq!(r.failed, 0);\n        assert_eq!(r.ignored, 1);\n        assert_eq!(r.filtered, 3);\n    }\n}"
}

Right. In dev2:

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/all.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)\npub fn print_test_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {\n    let mut passed = 0;\n    let mut failed = 0;\n\n    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results\n        .iter()\n        .map(|r| {\n            let detail = if r.success {\n                passed += 1;\n                \"[PASS]\".to_string()\n            } else {\n                failed += 1;\n                \"[FAIL]\".to_string()\n            };\n            SummaryRow {\n                name: r.name.clone(),\n                spec: r.spec.clone(),\n                detail,\n            }\n        })\n        .collect();\n\n    print_summary_table(\n        name,\n        \"TEST\",\n        \"Status\",\n        &rows,\n        &format!(\"Test: {passed} passed, {failed} failed\"),\n    );\n\n    if failed > 0 {\n        ExitCode::from(1)\n    } else {\n        ExitCode::SUCCESS\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)\npub fn print_test_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {\n    let mut pkg_passed = 0;\n    let mut pkg_failed = 0;\n    let mut total = TestResult::default();\n\n    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results\n        .iter()\n        .map(|r| {\n            let detail = if r.success {\n                pkg_passed += 1;\n                if let Some(counts) = &r.test_counts {\n                    total.merge(counts);\n                    format!(\"[PASS]  {} passed\", counts.passed)\n                } else {\n                    \"[PASS]\".to_string()\n                }\n            } else {\n                pkg_failed += 1;\n                if let Some(counts) = &r.test_counts {\n                    total.merge(counts);\n                    format!(\"[FAIL]  {} failed\", counts.failed)\n                } else {\n                    \"[FAIL]\".to_string()\n                }\n            };\n            SummaryRow {\n                name: r.name.clone(),\n                spec: r.spec.clone(),\n                detail,\n            }\n        })\n        .collect();\n\n    let pkg_count = pkg_passed + pkg_failed;\n    let footer = format!(\n        \"Test: {} packages, {} passed, {} failed\",\n        pkg_count, total.passed, total.failed\n    );\n\n    print_summary_table(name, \"TEST\", \"Status\", &rows, &footer);\n\n    if pkg_failed > 0 {\n        return ExitCode::from(1);\n    }\n\n    if total.total_ran() == 0 {\n        eprintln!(\"Error: 0 tests ran (filter may not match anything)\");\n        return ExitCode::from(1);\n    }\n\n    ExitCode::SUCCESS\n}"
}
```

```
     1→//! Tee utility: run a command, print stdout live, collect matching lines.
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     4→use std::io::{BufRead, BufReader};
     5→use std::process::{Command, ExitStatus, Stdio};
     6→
     7→/// Result of a tee'd command execution.
     8→pub struct TeeResult {
     9→    pub status: ExitStatus,
    10→    pub matched_lines: Vec<String>,
    11→}
    12→
    13→/// Spawn a command with piped stdout (stderr inherited),
    14→/// print each stdout line live, and collect lines where
    15→/// `filter(line)` returns true.
    16→pub fn tee_stdout<F>(cmd: &mut Command, filter: F) -> Result<TeeResult>
    17→where
    18→    F: Fn(&str) -> bool,
    19→{
    20→    let mut child = cmd
    21→        .stdout(Stdio::piped())
    22→        .stderr(Stdio::inherit())
    23→        .spawn()
    24→        .context("failed to spawn command")?;
    25→
    26→    let stdout = child.stdout.take().expect("stdout was piped");
    27→    let reader = BufReader::new(stdout);
    28→
    29→    let mut matched_lines = Vec::new();
    30→
    31→    for line in reader.lines() {
    32→        let line = line.context("failed to read stdout line")?;
    33→        if filter(&line) {
    34→            matched_lines.push(line.clone());
    35→        }
    36→        println!("{}", line);
    37→    }
    38→
    39→    let status = child.wait().context("failed to wait for command")?;
    40→
    41→    Ok(TeeResult {
    42→        status,
    43→        matched_lines,
    44→    })
    45→}
    46→

I'll add a `suppress` predicate parameter to `tee_stdout`. Lines where `suppress` returns true are not printed but are still collected if `filter` matches. This keeps `tee_stdout` generic:

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_012w231bzMqK6vsfmDtfdi4D
```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/tee.rs",
  "content": "//! Tee utility: run a command, print stdout live, collect matching lines.\n\nuse anyhow::{Context, Result};\nuse std::io::{BufRead, BufReader};\nuse std::process::{Command, ExitStatus, Stdio};\n\n/// Result of a tee'd command execution.\npub struct TeeResult {\n    pub status: ExitStatus,\n    pub matched_lines: Vec<String>,\n}\n\n/// Spawn a command with piped stdout (stderr inherited),\n/// print each stdout line live, and collect lines where\n/// `filter(line)` returns true. Lines where `suppress(line)`\n/// returns true are not printed (but still collected by filter).\npub fn tee_stdout<F, S>(cmd: &mut Command, filter: F, suppress: S) -> Result<TeeResult>\nwhere\n    F: Fn(&str) -> bool,\n    S: FnMut(&str) -> bool,\n{\n    let mut child = cmd\n        .stdout(Stdio::piped())\n        .stderr(Stdio::inherit())\n        .spawn()\n        .context(\"failed to spawn command\")?;\n\n    let stdout = child.stdout.take().expect(\"stdout was piped\");\n    let reader = BufReader::new(stdout);\n\n    let mut matched_lines = Vec::new();\n    let mut suppress = suppress;\n\n    for line in reader.lines() {\n        let line = line.context(\"failed to read stdout line\")?;\n        if filter(&line) {\n            matched_lines.push(line.clone());\n        }\n        if !suppress(&line) {\n            println!(\"{}\", line);\n        }\n    }\n\n    let status = child.wait().context(\"failed to wait for command\")?;\n\n    Ok(TeeResult {\n        status,\n        matched_lines,\n    })\n}\n"
}
```

Now update the `tee_stdout` call in `cargo_build.rs` with the noise suppression logic. The pattern is: suppress "running 0 tests" and the following empty result line (all zeros):

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01H6ai8ewfeX4h8Ceiehknm6
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cargo_build.rs",
  "old_string": "    // Execute the command — tee stdout for test mode to capture result lines\n    let (status, matched_lines) = if mode == CargoMode::Test {\n        let tee = tee_stdout(&mut cmd, |line| line.starts_with(\"test result:\"))\n            .with_context(|| format!(\"failed to run cargo {}\", mode.subcommand()))?;\n        (tee.status, tee.matched_lines)",
  "new_string": "    // Execute the command — tee stdout for test mode to capture result lines\n    let (status, matched_lines) = if mode == CargoMode::Test {\n        // Suppress \"running 0 tests\" noise and subsequent empty result lines\n        let mut saw_running_zero = false;\n        let tee = tee_stdout(\n            &mut cmd,\n            |line| line.starts_with(\"test result:\"),\n            |line| {\n                if line.trim() == \"running 0 tests\" {\n                    saw_running_zero = true;\n                    return true;\n                }\n                if saw_running_zero {\n                    saw_running_zero = false;\n                    // Suppress empty result line and blank line after \"running 0 tests\"\n                    if line.trim().is_empty()\n                        || line.starts_with(\"test result: ok. 0 passed; 0 failed;\")\n                    {\n                        return true;\n                    }\n                }\n                false\n            },\n        )\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"failed to run cargo {}\", mode.subcommand()))?;\n        (tee.status, tee.matched_lines)"
}
```

Wait — the "running 0 tests" for Doc-tests is still showing. That's because the suppress closure only suppresses the second empty block if it immediately follows "running 0 tests". But there's actually a blank line between "running 0 tests" and the result line in Doc-tests output. Let me check — actually, that's correct behavior. The Doc-tests "running 0 tests" block is showing because the closure resets `saw_running_zero` after one line. The blank line consumes it, then the result line isn't suppressed.

Let me fix the suppress logic to handle the sequence: "running 0 tests" → blank line → empty result line:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_017MjJ9jEw4mF3YGZyAVwRWE
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cargo_build.rs",
  "old_string": "        // Suppress \"running 0 tests\" noise and subsequent empty result lines\n        let mut saw_running_zero = false;\n        let tee = tee_stdout(\n            &mut cmd,\n            |line| line.starts_with(\"test result:\"),\n            |line| {\n                if line.trim() == \"running 0 tests\" {\n                    saw_running_zero = true;\n                    return true;\n                }\n                if saw_running_zero {\n                    saw_running_zero = false;\n                    // Suppress empty result line and blank line after \"running 0 tests\"\n                    if line.trim().is_empty()\n                        || line.starts_with(\"test result: ok. 0 passed; 0 failed;\")\n                    {\n                        return true;\n                    }\n                }\n                false\n            },\n        )",
  "new_string": "        // Suppress \"running 0 tests\" noise and subsequent empty result lines.\n        // Cargo emits: \"running 0 tests\" → \"\" → \"test result: ok. 0 passed; ...\"\n        let mut suppressing_zero_block = false;\n        let tee = tee_stdout(\n            &mut cmd,\n            |line| line.starts_with(\"test result:\"),\n            |line| {\n                if line.trim() == \"running 0 tests\" {\n                    suppressing_zero_block = true;\n                    return true;\n                }\n                if suppressing_zero_block {\n                    // Keep suppressing blank lines and the empty result line\n                    if line.trim().is_empty() {\n                        return true;\n                    }\n                    if line.starts_with(\"test result: ok. 0 passed; 0 failed;\") {\n                        suppressing_zero_block = false;\n                        return true;\n                    }\n                    // Non-blank, non-result line — stop suppressing\n                    suppressing_zero_block = false;\n                }\n                false\n            },\n        )"
}
```

The 0-test check is working but too aggressively — `pows_tspec_test_all_from_root` runs `tspec test` on a fixture workspace where the packages have no actual tests. That's legitimate (fixture packages don't need tests), but the 0-test guard is now failing them.

The 0-test guard should only apply to single-package mode (where the user explicitly targeted a package), not workspace/all-packages mode where some packages having 0 tests is normal. Let me reconsider:

- **All-packages mode:** Total 0 across all packages should warn but the per-package 0 is fine
- **Single-package mode:** 0 tests should fail

Actually, even in all-packages mode, if the total is 0 that's a problem. But the fixture workspaces have no tests — that's by design. The issue is this integration test: it runs `tspec test` on a fixture workspace and the fixture has 0 tests. The integration test itself expects success.

The simplest fix: the 0-test guard in `print_test_summary` (all-packages mode) should only trigger when total across all packages is 0 AND there are no compilation failures. But actually, the fixture has 0 tests legitimately. Let me re-read the integration test to understand:

     1→# Chores 6
     2→
     3→## 20260220 - Parse cargo test output for counts and filtering
     4→
     5→### Problem
     6→
     7→`tspec test` streams cargo output live but has no insight into what
     8→happened. The summary shows only `[PASS]`/`[FAIL]` per package with
     9→no test counts. Worse, 0 tests running reports as success — a filter
    10→typo silently passes. And `--test <target>` emits noisy "running 0
    11→tests" blocks for non-matching targets within a package.
    12→
    13→### Solution
    14→
    15→Tee stdout line-by-line (live output + selective capture), parse
    16→cargo's `test result:` lines for counts, and surface them:
    17→
    18→- Per-package counts in summary: `[PASS]  263 passed`
    19→- Aggregate footer: `Test: 2 packages, 279 passed, 0 failed`
    20→- Fail (exit 1) when 0 tests ran across all packages
    21→- Filter "running 0 tests" noise
    22→
    23→Infrastructure in a reusable `tee.rs` module so future commands
    24→(build warnings, benchmarks) can use the same pattern.
    25→
    26→### Plan
    27→
    28→- **dev1:** tee.rs + TestResult + parse_test_result_line + wire into
    29→  run_cargo for test mode. No visible behavior change.
    30→- **dev2:** Summary counts, 0-test failure, noise filtering.
```

> AGENT

The 0-test guard works. But there's a problem — it ran twice (the old binary is installed, running the test binary which also has the same code). The blank lines from suppressed "running 0 tests" blocks are still showing as empty output between `Running` headers. Those `Running` lines come from stderr, but the blank lines between them are from stdout.

Anyway, to your point about testing failures more robustly — we could:

1. **Add unit tests** for `print_test_summary` with mock `OpResult` data (both pass and fail cases with test_counts)
2. **Add an integration test** using a fixture with a deliberately failing test

Option 1 is cleaner and doesn't require new fixtures. We can construct `OpResult` vectors with `test_counts` and verify the exit code from `print_test_summary`. The summary itself prints to stdout (hard to capture in unit tests), but we can at least verify the exit code logic.

Option 2 would mean adding a `#[test] fn always_fails() { panic!(); }` to a fixture, but that makes the fixture itself always fail, complicating other tests that use it.

I'd recommend option 1 — unit tests for the summary logic with synthetic OpResult data. Want me to do that?

> AGENT

Committed `2ff21ad`.

Remember to commit `.claude/` session files.

For dev3 — what kind of failure testing did you have in mind? Options:

1. **Unit tests** for `print_test_summary` with synthetic OpResult data (pass/fail/mixed with counts, verify exit codes)
2. **Integration tests** with fixture that has a failing test (needs a new fixture or a flag to force failure)
3. Both

> DEVELOPER

both, but the integration tests should dynamically create the failing cases by copying the fixture. Although I'd like to be able to test manually. Thoughts?

> AGENT

For integration tests — copy POP fixture to tmpdir, inject a failing test file (`tests/fail_test.rs` with `#[test] fn fail() { panic!(); }`), run `tspec test`, verify exit code and summary output.

For manual testing, two options:

1. **A helper script** (`tests/manual_fail_test.sh`) that copies a fixture to `/tmp`, injects a failing test, and tells you to `cd /tmp/pop && tspec test`
2. **A persistent fixture** (`tests/fixtures/pop-fail/`) that's a copy of POP with a failing test baked in — you can just `cd tests/fixtures/pop-fail && tspec test` anytime

Option 2 is simpler for manual use, but it's a workspace-excluded fixture that permanently has a failing test. Option 1 is more disposable.

Or — simplest — just document the manual steps in chores-6.md and skip the script/fixture. The integration test does the real validation; manual testing is just `cp -r tests/fixtures/pop /tmp/pop-fail && echo '...' > /tmp/pop-fail/tests/fail_test.rs && cd /tmp/pop-fail && tspec test`.

Which approach do you prefer?

> DEVELOPER

What about having the failing tests as separate repos, just thinking?

> AGENT

You mean like `tests/fixtures/pop-fail/` and `tests/fixtures/pows-fail/` — separate fixtures that have failing tests baked in, permanently in the repo?

Pros:
- Dead simple to test manually: `cd tests/fixtures/pop-fail && tspec test`
- Integration tests just use `copy_fixture("pop-fail")` — no injection logic
- Each fixture is a self-contained, inspectable project

Cons:
- More fixtures to maintain (but they're tiny — just a Cargo.toml + a failing test)
- They always fail when run, so they must stay workspace-excluded (already the case for all fixtures)

I think it's clean. The fixtures would be minimal:

- `pop-fail/` — POP with one passing and one failing test (shows mixed summary)
- `pows-fail/` — POWS workspace where one member passes and one fails (shows per-package pass/fail in summary)

That covers both single-package and workspace failure paths. Want me to go with this?

> DEVELOPER

I was actually thinking in external repos but included is better under "normal" circumstances the `*-fail` are ignored and a flag is used during testing to use them?

> AGENT

Right — `#[ignore]` is perfect for this. The fail-fixture integration tests would:

- Be skipped during normal `tspec test` 
- Run explicitly with `tspec test -- --ignored` (or `--include-ignored` for everything)
- Always **pass** themselves — they assert tspec correctly returns exit 1 and shows the right summary

So `pop-fail` and `pows-fail` sit in `tests/fixtures/`, workspace-excluded, and the integration tests that use them are `#[ignore]`'d. When you're working on test output parsing you run `--ignored` to exercise them. Otherwise they stay out of the way.

For manual testing you just `cd tests/fixtures/pop-fail && tspec test` and see the output live.

Want me to proceed with this as dev3?

> DEVELOPER

In-tree is better\nfor normal development. Fail fixtures (`pop-fail`, `pows-fail`)\nlive in `tests/fixtures/`, workspace-excluded, with actual failing\ntests baked in. Integration tests using them are `#[ignore]`'d —\nskipped during normal `tspec test`, run with `-- --ignored`.\n\nManual testing: `cd tests/fixtures/pop-fail && tspec test`.\n\nAlso add unit tests for `print_test_summary` with synthetic\nOpResult data (pass/fail/mixed with counts, verify exit codes).\n\n### Result\n\n- `tee.rs` — reusable tee_stdout(cmd, filter, suppress) utility\n- `cargo_build.rs` — run_cargo returns raw matched lines for Test\n  mode; noise suppression for \"running 0 tests\" blocks\n- `cmd/test.rs` — TestResult, parse_test_result_line(),\n  parse_test_results(); 0-test guard for single-package mode\n- `all.rs` — OpResult.test_counts, per-package counts in summary,\n  aggregate footer\n- 0-test guard only in single-package mode (workspace fixtures\n  legitimately have 0 tests)"
}
```

     1→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     2→use tempfile::TempDir;
     3→
     4→/// Directory containing fixture projects.
     5→fn fixtures_dir() -> PathBuf {
     6→    PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("tests/fixtures")
     7→}
     8→
     9→/// Copy a fixture project into a fresh temporary directory.
    10→///
    11→/// Returns `(TempDir, PathBuf)` — the temp dir guard (drop removes it) and the
    12→/// path to the copied project root inside the temp dir.
    13→pub fn copy_fixture(name: &str) -> (TempDir, PathBuf) {
    14→    let src = fixtures_dir().join(name);
    15→    assert!(src.is_dir(), "fixture not found: {}", src.display());
    16→
    17→    let tmp = TempDir::new().expect("failed to create temp dir");
    18→    let dst = tmp.path().join(name);
    19→    copy_dir_recursive(&src, &dst).expect("failed to copy fixture");
    20→    (tmp, dst)
    21→}
    22→
    23→/// Recursively copy a directory, skipping `target/` subdirectories.
    24→fn copy_dir_recursive(src: &Path, dst: &Path) -> std::io::Result<()> {
    25→    std::fs::create_dir_all(dst)?;
    26→    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(src)? {
    27→        let entry = entry?;
    28→        let file_name = entry.file_name();
    29→        if file_name == "target" {
    30→            continue;
    31→        }
    32→        let src_path = entry.path();
    33→        let dst_path = dst.join(&file_name);
    34→        if src_path.is_dir() {
    35→            copy_dir_recursive(&src_path, &dst_path)?;
    36→        } else {
    37→            std::fs::copy(&src_path, &dst_path)?;
    38→        }
    39→    }
    40→    Ok(())
    41→}
    42→
```

```
     1→# tspec - Translation Spec Build System
     2→
     3→A spec-driven build system for Rust that wraps cargo with configurable target triples, compiler flags, and linker options. Each tspec applies to a Cargo **package** — the unit with a `Cargo.toml`. If different crates need different compilation settings, they should be in separate packages.
     4→
     5→**Status:** Active development. Works with both workspaces and single-package projects (i.e. Plain Old Packages, POPs).
     6→
     7→## Installation
     8→
     9→```bash
    10→cargo install --path .
    11→```
    12→
    13→Or run directly:
    14→```bash
    15→cargo run -- build
    16→```
    17→
    18→## Contributing
    19→
    20→**All non-trivial changes must be on a feature branch** — never commit directly to `main`. This applies even for solo development.
    21→
    22→```bash
    23→git checkout -b feat-my-feature    # Create a branch
    24→# ... make changes, commit ...
    25→git checkout main                  # Switch back
    26→git merge --no-ff feat-my-feature  # Merge with merge commit (or squash-merge)
    27→git push
    28→```
    29→
    30→Branch naming: `<type>-<description>` where type is `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `chore`.
    31→
    32→**Commits:** Conventional commits with the 50/72 rule — subject under 50 chars, body wrapped at 72 (the limit, not a target). Append `, vX.Y.Z` to the subject only on release commits.
    33→
    34→**Versioning:** Single-step changes bump directly (e.g., `0.14.0` → `0.15.0`). Multi-step features use a `-devN` series: `-dev0` for the marker/planning commit, then `-dev1`, `-dev2`, etc. for each code step, dropping the suffix on release.
    35→
    36→**Protect main on GitHub:** Enable branch protection (Settings > Branches) to block force pushes and optionally require PRs.
    37→
    38→For the full feature workflow (version bumps, notes, merge steps, Claude Code conventions), see [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md#feature-workflow).
    39→
    40→## Usage
    41→
    42→```bash
    43→tspec build                                # Build current package (or all in workspace) with tspec.ts.toml if present
    44→tspec build -p myapp                       # Build specific package with tspec.ts.toml if present
    45→tspec build myapp                          # Same, using positional argument
    46→tspec build .                              # Build the package in current directory
    47→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt          # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    48→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt.ts.toml  # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    49→tspec build -p myapp -r -s                 # Build release, strip symbols with tspec.ts.toml if present
    50→tspec run -p myapp                         # Build and run with tspec.ts.toml if present
    51→tspec test -p myapp                        # Build and test with tspec.ts.toml if present
    52→tspec build -w                             # Build all packages (even from inside a package dir)
    53→tspec build -t 'tspec*'                    # Build all packages using matching specs (quote the glob!)
    54→tspec build -t 'tspec-opt*'               # Build all packages with tspec-opt specs only
    55→tspec compare -p myapp                     # Compare binary sizes for a package
    56→tspec compare -p myapp -t *.ts.toml        # Compare using shell-expanded glob
    57→tspec compare                              # Compare all packages (workspace mode)
    58→tspec compare -w                           # Force workspace mode from inside a package
    59→tspec compare -w -f                        # Workspace mode, stop on first failure
    60→tspec -v build .                           # Verbose: show cargo command/env + cargo -v
    61→tspec -vv build .                          # Debug: also show spec details + cargo -vv
    62→tspec -j4 build .                          # Limit cargo to 4 parallel jobs
    63→```
    64→
    65→The `-p` flag or positional argument specifies a package by name or path (defaults to current directory if in a package, otherwise all packages). Paths like `.` are resolved to the actual cargo package name. At a pure workspace root (no `[package]`), `.` means "all packages."
    66→Use `-w, --workspace` to force all-packages mode even when inside a package directory.
    67→The `-t` flag selects a tspec file; if omitted and `tspec.ts.toml` exists, it's used automatically. In workspace mode (no `-p`, or with `-w`), `-t` patterns are resolved per-package — each sub-package is searched for matching specs, and packages with no matches are skipped.
    68→
    69→**Global flags** apply to all commands and are passed through to cargo: `-v` (cargo `-v`, shows rustc invocations), `-vv` (cargo `-vv`, full command details), and `-j N` (parallel jobs). tspec's own verbose output (command line, env vars, spec resolution) is also controlled by `-v`/`-vv`.
    70→
    71→**Important: quote glob patterns with `-t`.** The shell expands unquoted globs *before* tspec sees them. At a workspace root, `tspec build -t tspec*` may expand to literal filenames from the root directory (or worse, match `target/`), rather than being matched per-package. Always quote: `-t 'tspec*'`. tspec will warn if it detects shell-expanded non-tspec arguments.
    72→
    73→**Note on `cmd` vs `cmd .`:** For passthrough commands (clean, clippy, fmt), `tspec cmd` passes no `-p` to cargo (operates on everything), while `tspec cmd .` resolves to `cargo cmd -p <name>` (package-specific). The difference is most visible with `clean`: `cargo clean` removes all of target/ while `cargo clean -p <name>` leaves shared metadata files. This matches cargo's own behavior. Use `tspec clean` or `tspec clean -w` for a full clean.
    74→
    75→## tspec Files
    76→
    77→Translation specs are TOML files (conventionally `*.ts.toml`) that configure builds:
    78→
    79→```toml
    80→# tspec.ts.toml - all supported fields shown
    81→
    82→# Top-level high-level options (expand to lower-level cargo/rustc flags)
    83→panic = "abort"                            # "unwind" (default), "abort", "immediate-abort" (nightly)
    84→strip = "symbols"                          # "none" (default), "debuginfo", "symbols"
    85→toolchain = "nightly"                      # override toolchain (e.g., "nightly", "stable", "1.75")
    86→rustflags = ["-C", "relocation-model=static"]  # raw flags passed through to RUSTFLAGS
    87→
    88→[cargo]
    89→profile = "release"                        # "debug" (default), "release", or custom profile
    90→target_triple = "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl"
    91→target_json = "path/to/custom-target.json" # custom target spec (auto-adds -Z json-target-spec)
    92→unstable = ["panic-immediate-abort"]       # -Z flags (nightly only)
    93→target_dir = "{name}"                      # per-spec target dir; supports {name} and {hash} placeholders
    94→build_std = ["core", "alloc"]              # crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly)
    95→
    96→[cargo.config.profile.release]             # nested tables under [cargo.config] become individual
    97→opt-level = "z"                            # --config KEY=VALUE args to cargo; any valid cargo
    98→lto = true                                 # config key works (profile, build, target, etc.)
    99→codegen-units = 1
   100→
   101→[linker]
   102→args = ["-static", "-nostartfiles"]
   103→
   104→[linker.version_script]                    # symbol visibility control (enables --gc-sections)
   105→global = ["_start"]                        # symbols to keep
   106→local = "*"                                # default: "*" (hide everything else)
   107→```
   108→
   109→The top-level `panic` sets both cargo `-Z` and rustc `-C` flags automatically.
   110→
   111→### Key Concepts
   112→
   113→- **One tspec per package** - A tspec applies to all crates (targets) within a Cargo package. For per-crate control, use separate packages in a workspace.
   114→- **tspec.ts.toml is optional** - Packages without one get plain `cargo build/test`
   115→- **Generated build.rs** - tspec generates temporary build.rs for scoped linker flags
   116→- **Spec comparison** - Compare binary sizes across different specs; always shows both `cargo --release` and `cargo --release-strip` baselines. Supports `-w` for all-packages mode — per-package tables are collected and printed together at the end. With a single package, only its comparison table is shown
   117→
   118→### How Linker Flags Work
   119→
   120→tspec generates a temporary `build.rs` to apply linker flags with binary-scoped directives:
   121→
   122→```rust
   123→// Generated by tspec - deleted after build
   124→fn main() {
   125→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-static");
   126→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-nostdlib");
   127→}
   128→```
   129→
   130→The only other alternative is to add these linker flags to RUSTFLAGS, but that
   131→affects ALL compilations (including dependency build scripts), causing crashes
   132→with flags like `-nostartfiles`. The generated build.rs scopes flags to just
   133→the binary.
   134→
   135→### Cargo Config Passthrough (`cargo.config`)
   136→
   137→The `[cargo.config]` section is a general-purpose passthrough to cargo's `--config KEY=VALUE` mechanism. Any nested tables under `[cargo.config]` are flattened into dotted keys and passed as `--config` args. This lets you override any [cargo configuration](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/config.html) value at the command line.
   138→
   139→**Profile settings** are the most common use case:
   140→
   141→```toml
   142→[cargo.config.profile.release]
   143→opt-level = "z"
   144→codegen-units = 1
   145→lto = true
   146→```
   147→
   148→This produces: `cargo --config profile.release.opt-level="z" --config profile.release.codegen-units=1 --config profile.release.lto=true`
   149→
   150→**Other cargo config keys** work too:
   151→
   152→```toml
   153→[cargo.config.target.x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu]
   154→linker = "clang"
   155→runner = "qemu-x86_64"
   156→```
   157→
   158→**Note:** Do not use `build.rustflags` in `[cargo.config]`. tspec sets the `RUSTFLAGS` env var (from top-level `rustflags`, `panic`, and `strip`), which takes precedence over `--config build.rustflags` per cargo's precedence rules. Use the top-level `rustflags` array instead.
   159→
   160→**Custom profiles:** Any profile defined in your workspace `Cargo.toml` (with `inherits`) can be used. tspec validates that custom profiles exist before invoking cargo. Use `cargo.profile` in the spec or `--profile` on the CLI.
   161→
   162→**Flat dotted keys** also work — TOML treats them equivalently:
   163→
   164→```toml
   165→[cargo.config]
   166→"profile.release.opt-level" = "z"
   167→```
   168→
   169→## tspec Management (ts subcommand)
   170→
   171→Manage spec files without manual TOML editing. All commands that modify files preserve comments and formatting.
   172→
   173→| Command | Description |
   174→|---------|-------------|
   175→| `ts list` | List tspec files found in the workspace or a specific package |
   176→| `ts show` | Display the TOML contents of a tspec file |
   177→| `ts hash` | Print the content hash (used in backup filenames) of a tspec |
   178→| `ts new` | Create a new tspec with defaults, or copy an existing one with `-f` (byte-for-byte) |
   179→| `ts set` | Set a scalar value or replace an entire array |
   180→| `ts unset` | Remove a field (scalar or array) from a tspec entirely |
   181→| `ts add` | Add items to an array field (append or insert at position) |
   182→| `ts remove` | Remove items from an array field (by value or by index) |
   183→| `ts backup` | Create a versioned backup copy (`name-NNN-hash.ts.toml`) |
   184→| `ts restore` | Restore a tspec by copying a versioned backup back to its original name |
   185→
   186→```bash
   187→# List / show / hash
   188→tspec ts list                            # List all tspec files
   189→tspec ts list -p myapp                   # List tspec files for a package
   190→tspec ts show -p myapp                   # Show all tspec contents
   191→tspec ts show -p myapp -t tspec-opt      # Show specific tspec-opt.ts.toml
   192→tspec ts hash -p myapp                   # Show content hash of tspec.ts.toml
   193→
   194→# Create new tspec files
   195→tspec ts new -p myapp                    # Create tspec.ts.toml with defaults
   196→tspec ts new experiment -p myapp         # Create experiment.ts.toml
   197→tspec ts new opt2 -p myapp -f tspec-opt  # Copy from existing spec (byte-for-byte)
   198→
   199→# Set scalar values
   200→tspec ts set strip symbols -p myapp              # Set a scalar field
   201→tspec ts set cargo.profile release               # No quoting needed
   202→
   203→# Set (replace) entire arrays — each element is a separate arg
   204→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib        # Replace array
   205→tspec ts set cargo.build_std core alloc           # Replace array
   206→tspec ts set rustflags -C relocation-model=static # Replace array
   207→
   208→# Add items to arrays (append by default)
   209→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections        # Append one item
   210→tspec ts add linker.args -nostdlib -pie            # Append multiple items
   211→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles        # Insert at position 0
   212→
   213→# Remove items from arrays
   214→tspec ts remove linker.args -nostdlib              # Remove by value
   215→tspec ts remove linker.args -static -pie           # Remove multiple by value
   216→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args                   # Remove by index
   217→
   218→# Remove a field entirely
   219→tspec ts unset strip -p myapp                      # Remove scalar field
   220→tspec ts unset linker.args -t tspec-opt            # Remove array field
   221→
   222→# Backup and restore (byte-for-byte copies)
   223→tspec ts backup -p myapp                 # Backup tspec.ts.toml → tspec-001-abcd1234.ts.toml
   224→tspec ts backup -p myapp -t tspec-opt    # Backup tspec-opt.ts.toml
   225→tspec ts restore -t t1-001-abcd1234      # Restore t1.ts.toml from backup
   226→```
   227→
   228→Backups are valid spec files and can be used directly with `-t`.
   229→
   230→`ts set`, `ts unset`, `ts add`, and `ts remove` use `toml_edit` for surgical editing — comments and formatting are preserved.
   231→`ts backup`, `ts restore`, and `ts new -f` use byte-for-byte file copy — comments are preserved exactly.
   232→
   233→### Supported keys
   234→
   235→Keys use two forms: `FIELD` for top-level fields (`panic`, `strip`, `rustflags`) or `TABLE.FIELD` for nested fields (`cargo.profile`, `linker.args`).
   236→
   237→| Key | Type | Values |
   238→|-----|------|--------|
   239→| `panic` | scalar | `unwind`, `abort`, `immediate-abort` |
   240→| `strip` | scalar | `none`, `debuginfo`, `symbols` |
   241→| `toolchain` | scalar | e.g. `nightly`, `stable`, `nightly-2025-01-15`, `1.75` |
   242→| `rustflags` | array | e.g. `-C relocation-model=static` |
   243→| `cargo.profile` | scalar | `debug`, `release`, or any custom profile defined in `Cargo.toml` |
   244→| `cargo.target_triple` | scalar | any string |
   245→| `cargo.target_json` | scalar | any path |
   246→| `cargo.target_dir` | scalar | any string (supports `{name}`, `{hash}`) |
   247→| `cargo.unstable` | array | e.g. `panic-immediate-abort` |
   248→| `cargo.config` | table | arbitrary `--config KEY=VALUE` passthrough; supports nested tables (e.g. `[cargo.config.profile.release]`) |
   249→| `cargo.build_std` | array | e.g. `core alloc` |
   250→| `linker.args` | array | e.g. `-static -nostdlib` |
   251→
   252→### Array operations
   253→
   254→Four orthogonal commands operate on arrays at two levels:
   255→
   256→| Level | Command | Purpose |
   257→|-------|---------|---------|
   258→| Field-level | `ts set` | Replace entire array with new values |
   259→| Field-level | `ts unset` | Remove the array field entirely |
   260→| Item-level | `ts add` | Append items (or insert at position with `-i`) |
   261→| Item-level | `ts remove` | Remove items by value (or by index with `-i`) |
   262→
   263→```bash
   264→tspec ts set linker.args -static -nostdlib   # replace all with these
   265→tspec ts unset linker.args                   # clear the field
   266→tspec ts add linker.args -Wl,--gc-sections   # append (skips duplicates)
   267→tspec ts add -i 0 linker.args -nostartfiles  # insert at position
   268→tspec ts remove linker.args -static          # remove by value
   269→tspec ts remove -i 2 linker.args             # remove by index
   270→```
   271→
   272→Values starting with `-` work without quoting thanks to `allow_hyphen_values`.
   273→Use `--` as an escape hatch if a value collides with a flag like `-p`, `-t`, or `-i`.
   274→
   275→Note: Use `ts` as the subcommand (short for "tspec management").
   276→
   277→## Testing
   278→
   279→```bash
   280→# Basic usage
   281→tspec test -p tspec                        # Run tspec tests
   282→tspec test -p tspec-build                  # Run tspec-build tests
   283→tspec test -w                              # Run all workspace tests
   284→
   285→# Filter by test function name (-n/--names, substring match, OR-matched)
   286→tspec test -p tspec -n flatten_config      # Run tests matching "flatten_config"
   287→tspec test -p tspec -n flatten config      # Run tests matching "flatten" OR "config"
   288→
   289→# Select test target (--test, basename of files in tests/ without .rs)
   290→tspec test --test tspec_test               # Run only tests/tspec_test.rs
   291→tspec test --test foo --test bar           # Run multiple test files
   292→tspec test --test tspec_test -n load_spec  # Combine: target + name filter
   293→
   294→# Discovery
   295→tspec test -p tspec --target-names         # List available names for --test
   296→tspec test -p tspec --list                 # List all test functions grouped by target
   297→tspec test -p tspec -n flatten --list      # List functions matching a name filter
   298→
   299→# Target selection
   300→tspec test --test tspec_test               # Run only the tspec_test target
   301→```
   302→
   303→**Test flags:**
   304→- `-n, --names <FILTER>...` — match anywhere in the qualified test name (e.g., `-n remove` matches `ts_cmd::remove::tests::remove_by_index`; multiple filters are OR-matched)
   305→- `--test <TARGET-NAME>` — run only the named test target (the basename of a file in `tests/` without `.rs`; repeatable). In workspace mode, only packages containing the target are tested. Use `--target-names` to see available names
   306→- `--target-names` — list available target names for `--test`
   307→- `--list` / `-l` — list all test functions grouped by target
   308→- `-- <ARGS>` — pass trailing arguments to the test binary (e.g., `--ignored`, `--exact`, `--nocapture`)
   309→
   310→## Project Structure
   311→
   312→```
   313→tspec/                  # Workspace root (also the main tspec package)
   314→  Cargo.toml            # [workspace] + [package] — workspace with root package
   315→  src/
   316→    lib.rs              # Library root, exposes modules
   317→    main.rs             # Entry point, dispatch
   318→    cli.rs              # Clap CLI definitions
   319→    types.rs            # Spec parameter types
   320→    tspec.rs            # Spec loading/saving/hashing
   321→    find_paths.rs       # Project/package/tspec/binary path discovery
   322→    workspace.rs        # Workspace package discovery
   323→    cargo_build.rs      # Unified cargo runner (build + test) + generated build.rs
   324→    run.rs              # Run command implementation
   325→    compare.rs          # Compare command (size comparison)
   326→    all.rs              # Batch operations (build_all, run_all, test_all, compare_all)
   327→    cmd/                # Command implementations (one file per command)
   328→    ts_cmd/             # Tspec management subcommands
   329→    binary.rs           # Binary operations (strip, size)
   330→  tests/
   331→    data/               # Test fixtures (TOML specs)
   332→    tspec_test.rs       # Integration tests
   333→  tspec-build/          # Library crate for build.rs integration
   334→    src/lib.rs          # Reads TSPEC_SPEC_FILE env var at build time
   335→  notes/                # Design docs and todo tracking
   336→```
   337→
   338→## Development
   339→
   340→See [notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md) for current tasks.
   341→
   342→### Verification
   343→
   344→```bash
   345→tspec test -p tspec
   346→tspec test -p tspec-build
   347→tspec clippy
   348→tspec fmt --check
   349→```
   350→
   351→## Origin
   352→
   353→Originally developed as `xt` within the [rlibc-x](https://github.com/user/rlibc-x) project, then extracted to a standalone tool. The git history was preserved using `git subtree split`.
   354→
   355→## License
   356→
   357→Licensed under either of
   358→
   359→- Apache License, Version 2.0 ([LICENSE-APACHE](LICENSE-APACHE) or http://apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0)
   360→- MIT license ([LICENSE-MIT](LICENSE-MIT) or http://opensource.org/licenses/MIT)
   361→
   362→### Contribution
   363→
   364→Unless you explicitly state otherwise, any contribution intentionally submitted
   365→for inclusion in the work by you, as defined in the Apache-2.0 license, shall
   366→be dual licensed as above, without any additional terms or conditions.
   367→

> AGENT

Could you clarify — do you mean:

1. The fail-fixture tests should **not** be `#[ignore]`'d (run by default), or
2. We should document `-- --ignored` more prominently as the standard way to run failure-path tests, or
3. Something else?

> DEVELOPER

Actually, we need to be prominent that `tspec test` and `tspec test -p tspec` do not run all the tests and actually it'd be nice to have `tspec test --all-tests` == `tspec test -- include-ignored` personally, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good idea. `--all-tests` is much more discoverable than `-- --include-ignored`. Users shouldn't need to know cargo's internal test harness flags to run everything.

Implementation-wise, it's straightforward — when `--all-tests` is set, push `--include-ignored` into the test args that go after `--`. And in the README/docs, we make it clear that `tspec test` skips `#[ignore]`'d tests by default.

One question: should `--all-tests` have a short flag? `-a` is available and natural. Or keep it long-only since it's not used often?

> DEVELOPER

hmm, no `-a` for the moment, but this is "general" correct and useful for regular users and we should prominently document that?

> AGENT

Yes, exactly — `--all-tests` is general purpose. Any Rust project can have `#[ignore]`'d tests (slow tests, network tests, etc.), not just our fail fixtures. It's a convenience wrapper over cargo's `-- --include-ignored` that's more discoverable.

Let me add it and document it prominently.

    14→#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone)]
    15→pub struct TestResult {
    16→    pub passed: u32,
    17→    pub failed: u32,
    18→    pub ignored: u32,
    19→    pub filtered: u32,
    20→}
    21→
    22→impl TestResult {
    23→    /// Aggregate another TestResult into this one.
    24→    pub fn merge(&mut self, other: &TestResult) {
    25→        self.passed += other.passed;
    26→        self.failed += other.failed;
    27→        self.ignored += other.ignored;
    28→        self.filtered += other.filtered;
    29→    }
    30→
    31→    /// Total tests that actually ran (passed + failed).
    32→    pub fn total_ran(&self) -> u32 {
    33→        self.passed + self.failed
    34→    }
    35→}
    36→
    37→/// Parse a `test result:` line from cargo test output.
    38→///
    39→/// Example: `test result: ok. 263 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.42s`
    40→pub fn parse_test_result_line(line: &str) -> Option<TestResult> {
    41→    let rest = line.strip_prefix("test result:")?;
    42→    let rest = rest.trim();
    43→    // Skip the "ok." or "FAILED." prefix
    44→    let rest = rest
    45→        .strip_prefix("ok.")
    46→        .or_else(|| rest.strip_prefix("FAILED."))?;
    47→    let rest = rest.trim();
    48→
    49→    let mut result = TestResult::default();
    50→    for part in rest.split(';') {
    51→        let part = part.trim();
    52→        if let Some((num, label)) = part.split_once(' ') {
    53→            let Ok(n) = num.parse::<u32>() else {
    54→                continue;
    55→            };
    56→            match label.trim() {
    57→                "passed" => result.passed = n,
    58→                "failed" => result.failed = n,
    59→                "ignored" => result.ignored = n,
    60→                "filtered" | "filtered out" => result.filtered = n,
    61→                _ => {} // "measured"
    62→            }
    63→        }
    64→    }
    65→    Some(result)
    66→}
    67→
    68→/// Parse and aggregate raw `test result:` lines into a single TestResult.

  "new_string": "        // Build extra_args from --names, --test, --all-tests, and trailing args\n        let has_extra = !self.name_filter.is_empty()\n            || !self.test_target.is_empty()\n            || !self.test_args.is_empty()\n            || self.all_tests;\n        let flags = if has_extra {\n            let mut f = flags.clone();\n            for target in &self.test_target {\n                f.extra_args.push(\"--test\".to_string());\n                f.extra_args.push(target.clone());\n            }\n            // Name filters, --all-tests, and test_args all go after --\n            // The test harness accepts [FILTERS...] as OR-matched substrings\n            if !self.name_filter.is_empty() || !self.test_args.is_empty() || self.all_tests {\n                f.extra_args.push(\"--\".to_string());\n                f.extra_args.extend(self.name_filter.clone());\n                if self.all_tests {\n                    f.extra_args.push(\"--include-ignored\".to_string());\n                }\n                f.extra_args.extend(self.test_args.clone());\n            }\n            f\n        } else {\n            flags.clone()\n        };"

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/README.md",
  "old_string": "## Testing\n\n```bash\n# Basic usage\ntspec test -p tspec                        # Run tspec tests\ntspec test -p tspec-build                  # Run tspec-build tests\ntspec test -w                              # Run all workspace tests\n\n# Filter by test function name (-n/--names, substring match, OR-matched)\ntspec test -p tspec -n flatten_config      # Run tests matching \"flatten_config\"\ntspec test -p tspec -n flatten config      # Run tests matching \"flatten\" OR \"config\"\n\n# Select test target (--test, basename of files in tests/ without .rs)\ntspec test --test tspec_test               # Run only tests/tspec_test.rs\ntspec test --test foo --test bar           # Run multiple test files\ntspec test --test tspec_test -n load_spec  # Combine: target + name filter\n\n# Discovery\ntspec test -p tspec --target-names         # List available names for --test\ntspec test -p tspec --list                 # List all test functions grouped by target\ntspec test -p tspec -n flatten --list      # List functions matching a name filter\n\n# Target selection\ntspec test --test tspec_test               # Run only the tspec_test target\n```\n\n**Test flags:**\n- `-n, --names <FILTER>...` — match anywhere in the qualified test name (e.g., `-n remove` matches `ts_cmd::remove::tests::remove_by_index`; multiple filters are OR-matched)\n- `--test <TARGET-NAME>` — run only the named test target (the basename of a file in `tests/` without `.rs`; repeatable). In workspace mode, only packages containing the target are tested. Use `--target-names` to see available names\n- `--target-names` — list available target names for `--test`\n- `--list` / `-l` — list all test functions grouped by target\n- `-- <ARGS>` — pass trailing arguments to the test binary (e.g., `--ignored`, `--exact`, `--nocapture`)\n\n**Test summary** (workspace mode) shows per-package counts and an aggregate footer:\n\n```\n============================================\n             tspec TEST SUMMARY\n============================================\n  Package      Status\n  tspec-build  [PASS]  10 passed\n  tspec        [PASS]  293 passed\n\n  Test: 2 packages, 303 passed, 0 failed\n============================================\n```\n\nIn single-package mode, tspec fails (exit 1) when 0 tests ran — this catches filter typos that silently pass. \"Running 0 tests\" noise blocks are suppressed from output.\n\n**Fail fixtures** for testing failure output are in `tests/fixtures/pop-fail/` and `tests/fixtures/pows-fail/`. These have intentionally failing tests and can be run manually:\n\n```bash\ncd tests/fixtures/pop-fail && tspec test .    # Single-package failure\ncd tests/fixtures/pows-fail && tspec test     # Workspace mixed pass/fail\n```\n\nIntegration tests using these fixtures are `#[ignore]`'d:\n\n```bash\ntspec test -- --ignored           # Run only fail-fixture tests\ntspec test -- --include-ignored   # Run all tests including fail-fixture\n```",
  "new_string": "## Testing\n\n**Important:** `tspec test` skips `#[ignore]`'d tests by default (same as `cargo test`). Use `--all-tests` to include them:\n\n```bash\ntspec test -p tspec                        # Run tspec tests (skips #[ignore]'d)\ntspec test -p tspec --all-tests            # Run ALL tests including #[ignore]'d\ntspec test --all-tests                     # Run all workspace tests including #[ignore]'d\n```\n\n```bash\n# Basic usage\ntspec test -p tspec                        # Run tspec tests\ntspec test -p tspec-build                  # Run tspec-build tests\ntspec test -w                              # Run all workspace tests\n\n# Filter by test function name (-n/--names, substring match, OR-matched)\ntspec test -p tspec -n flatten_config      # Run tests matching \"flatten_config\"\ntspec test -p tspec -n flatten config      # Run tests matching \"flatten\" OR \"config\"\n\n# Select test target (--test, basename of files in tests/ without .rs)\ntspec test --test tspec_test               # Run only tests/tspec_test.rs\ntspec test --test foo --test bar           # Run multiple test files\ntspec test --test tspec_test -n load_spec  # Combine: target + name filter\n\n# Discovery\ntspec test -p tspec --target-names         # List available names for --test\ntspec test -p tspec --list                 # List all test functions grouped by target\ntspec test -p tspec -n flatten --list      # List functions matching a name filter\n```\n\n**Test flags:**\n- `--all-tests` — include `#[ignore]`'d tests (equivalent to `-- --include-ignored`)\n- `-n, --names <FILTER>...` — match anywhere in the qualified test name (e.g., `-n remove` matches `ts_cmd::remove::tests::remove_by_index`; multiple filters are OR-matched)\n- `--test <TARGET-NAME>` — run only the named test target (the basename of a file in `tests/` without `.rs`; repeatable). In workspace mode, only packages containing the target are tested. Use `--target-names` to see available names\n- `--target-names` — list available target names for `--test`\n- `--list` / `-l` — list all test functions grouped by target\n- `-- <ARGS>` — pass trailing arguments to the test binary (e.g., `--ignored`, `--exact`, `--nocapture`)\n\n**Test summary** (workspace mode) shows per-package counts and an aggregate footer:\n\n```\n============================================\n             tspec TEST SUMMARY\n============================================\n  Package      Status\n  tspec-build  [PASS]  10 passed\n  tspec        [PASS]  293 passed\n\n  Test: 2 packages, 303 passed, 0 failed\n============================================\n```\n\nIn single-package mode, tspec fails (exit 1) when 0 tests ran — this catches filter typos that silently pass. \"Running 0 tests\" noise blocks are suppressed from output.\n\n**Fail fixtures** for testing failure output are in `tests/fixtures/pop-fail/` and `tests/fixtures/pows-fail/`. These have intentionally failing tests and can be run manually:\n\n```bash\ncd tests/fixtures/pop-fail && tspec test .    # Single-package failure\ncd tests/fixtures/pows-fail && tspec test     # Workspace mixed pass/fail\n```\n\nIntegration tests using fail fixtures are `#[ignore]`'d — run them with `--all-tests`:\n\n```bash\ntspec test --all-tests                     # Run everything including fail-fixture tests\ntspec test -- --ignored                    # Run ONLY the #[ignore]'d tests\n```"
}
